| `S2-14-const`: `for (const t of &TABLE) { *t = 0; }` with `private const TABLE: i64[3] = [1, 2, 3];`; `for (const x of pick(&v)) { *x = 7; }` with `let v: i64[] = [];` and a `pick(xs: &Slice<i64>): &Slice<i64>` that returns `&ZEROS`, a top-level `const`, for an empty view and `xs` otherwise; `const r = st.view(); for (const x of r) { *x = 0; }` with `public view(): readonly &i64[]` | EN0608 at `&TABLE`; EN0616 at `pick(&v)`, whose result may point at a constant; EN0616 at `r`. A loop whose binding is written anywhere in its body writes through its source, so the source is checked at the pre-header as an argument to a writing parameter is, and its mark is reported once, at the source (twin E0596 `` cannot borrow immutable static item `ZEROS` as mutable `` and E0594 `` cannot assign to `*x`, which is behind a `&` reference ``) |
| A returned element with no `_src`: `public function f(xs: &Slice<T>): &T { for (const x of xs) { if (c(x)) { return x; } } panic("none"); }`, with a caller that writes through the result | Compiles; `held(x)` includes `Param(0)`. The `get_mut` twin loops over `xs.iter_mut()` by the translation of section 13 and compiles too, as a twin that returns `Some(x)` from inside a loop over `iter_mut()` compiles and prints `[1, 0, 3, 7]`; the saved-reference form that the translation once used was rejected (E0499, and E0502 from `xs.len()` at the head) (`TK-02`) |
| The tour, `Ownership in three lines`, S5's `longest = w`, and S5's `for (const n of &nums) { *n *= 2; }` | Compile with no warning |
| `public class H { public items: string[] \| null = null; }` with `h: &H` and `if (h.items != null) { for (const s of &h.items) { wipe(h); } }` | EN0706 at `wipe(h)`, and EN0401 at the source naming it; the same for `wipe(this)`, `f(h)` through a closure parameter, and `h?.wipe()` |
| With `h: &H`: `const g = h; g.items = null;` and `const { items } = h; *items = null;` then a read of `h.items`, with no loop | EN0401 at the read, naming the write, not a tag panic |
| Under `p != null`: `for (const x of &p) { p = null; }`, and in place of `p = null`, `p.take()`, `mem.replace(&p, null)`, a write in an arrow, `take(p)`, a change before `continue` | Rejected by the hold, EN0706 or EN0709, with EN0401 at the source beside it where a recorded reference writes (N2), as for `wipe(h)`; never a tag panic. A write in an arrow is a visible change, which kills the fact at the call (SPEC 4.8, Arrows) and records nothing, so it is EN0706 alone. `mem.replace(&p, null)` is EN0401 in phase 2 instead, at `null`: under the fact `&p` has type `&string[]`, which cannot store `null` (SPEC 4.8, Effect), so the hold is never checked |
| Under `p != null`: `for (const x of &p) { p = null; break; }`; `for (const x of &p) { p = null; if (p == null) { break; } }` | Compiles; EN0706, because the test's false branch reaches the next head after the assignment |
| Under `p != null`, with `p: string[] \| null`: `for (const x of &p) { p = null; break; } console.log(p.length());`; the same with `const r: &string[] = &p;` as the read | EN0303; EN0401. The `break`'s state lacks the fact, so the join after the loop does too, and this never reaches a `CheckTag` panic |
| Under `p != null`, with `p: string[] \| null`: `outer: for (const a of &p) { for (const x of &p) { p = null; break outer; } } console.log(p.length());`; the same with `const r: &string[] = &p;` as the read | EN0303; EN0401. `break outer` records its state for `outer` |
| Under `p != null`: `for (const x of &p) { if (c) { p = null; break; } } console.log(p.length());`; `for (const x of &p) { p = null; if (p == null) { break; } } console.log(p.length());` | EN0303 each, and EN0706 at the second's assignment, as above. One `break` without the fact is enough, even when the back edge has it |
| Under `p != null`: `outer: for (const a of &p) { while (c) { p = null; break outer; } }` | Compiles with no warning. The prescan of the `while` kills the fact at its own head, so the back edge of `outer` lacks it, but a source fact is not checked at the back edges: the hold protects it, and no next head of `outer` follows the assignment |
| Under `p != null`: `for (const x of &p) { if (c) { break; } } console.log(p.length());`; `for (const x of &p) { if (c) { p = null; return; } } console.log(p.length());`; `outer: for (const a of &p) { for (const x of &p) { if (c) { p = null; break outer; } } console.log(p.length()); }` | Compiles with no warning. Every `break` of the loop holds the fact, a `return` never reaches the join, and `break outer` is not a `break` of the inner loop |
| Under `p != null`: `for (const x of [...p]) { p = null; }`, `for (const x of &p) { *x = 1; }` with `let p` | Compiles |
| Under `o != null`: `for (const x of o.kids()) { o = null; }` with `public kids(): &T[]` | No EN0401, because the source is evaluated once. EN0706, because `_src` holds the method's loan on `o` |
| Under `p != null`: `for (const x of &p) { p.push(1); }` | EN0706 |
| `S2-24-narrowed`: `let keep: &i64 \| null = null; if (b.items != null) { for (const x of &b.items) { if (*x > 2) { keep = x; } } } if (keep != null) { *keep = 0; } console.log(b.items);` with `public class Bag { public items: i64[] \| null = null; }` and `b.items = [1, 5, 3, 7]`; `if (p != null) { for (const x of &p) { *x += 1; } }` with `let p: i64[] \| null = [1, 2, 3];`, then `console.log(p)`; `bump(xs?: &Slice<i64>)` running `if (xs != null) { for (const x of xs) { *x += 1; } }`, called as `bump(v.slice(0, 3))` on `let v: i64[] = [1, 2, 3]`, then `console.log(v)` | Compile, and print `[1, 5, 3, 0]`, `[2, 3, 4]` and `[2, 3, 4]`: the `CheckTag` and `CheckBounds` runs of a loop's head and bind are part of that head and bind, so they skip the hold and the loop's own turn keys as its `Len` and `Ref` do, and never give EN0708 (twin prints `Some([1, 5, 3, 0])` and `[2, 3, 4]`) |
| `.mir`: a narrowed head, with both `CheckTag` and `CheckBounds` before the projection | Passes the verifier; a statement between them fails it |
| `let xs = [new M()]; for (let i = 0; i < xs.length(); i++) { xs.push(new M()); } public final class M {}` under fuel | T0004; the zero-sized `M` at `length() == isize.MAX` on `vm32` panics `capacity overflow`. `M` is `final`, because a class that can be extended carries a method-table pointer and is not zero-sized |
| Spread: `[...a, f(&a)]`, `[f(&a), ...a]`, `a = [...a, x]` | Compiles; order as SPEC 4.13 |
| `[...a, g()?]` with `g` failing | `Err` returned, the built part dropped, `allocs: balanced` |
| `[...move a]`; `a.extendCloned(&a)` | EN0228; EN0706, the receiver borrow is taken last |
| `const f: i32[3] = [1, 2, 3]; const c = [...f];`, `[...&f]`, `public function g(a: &i32[3]): i32[] { return [...a]; }`, `[...&q]` on a `let` or a `const`, `[...r]` with `r: &T[]` then a use of `r` | Compiles |
| `const r = &f; const c = [...f]; r[0] = 1;` with `let f` | EN0708 |
| `[...b]` with `b: Box<T[]>`, `[...5]`, `[..."abc"]`, `[...o]` with `o: T[] \| null` | EN0438, with a `...*b` help on the first |
| `const a: i64[3] = [...b];`, `const n: (i64 \| null)[] = [...xs];`, `[0, ...xs]` at `(i64 \| null)[]`, `[...a, ...b]` of `i32[]` and `i64[]` | EN0401, the second and third with a `.map` help |
| `let q; const c = [...q]; q = [1];`; `let q = []; const c = [...q]; q.push(1);`; `[...[]]` with no expected type | EN0410; compiles; EN0410 |
| `<T>(xs: &T[])`, `T extends Clone`, `T extends Copy`, each with `[...xs]` | EN0438 (G22); compiles; compiles |
| Elements: `i64`, `(*u8)[]`, a Copy class with a raw pointer, a class with a `Box<Node> \| null` field, `T \| null`, `i64[2][]`, `Box<i64>[]`, `Result<i64>[]` | Compiles; a `.clone()` on each resolves too |
| Elements: a class with a destructor and no `clone`, a non-Copy class with a raw pointer field; a class with a destructor and a `clone` | EN0438; compiles |
| `private const Y = [...X];`, an `unsafe let` initialiser, `RepeatLit` count, `T[N]` length, a top-level `assert` in a file with no statements, each spreading | EN0412 with the note, before any EN0401 |
| `const ys = [...xs];` in a function, and a field initialiser `public items: i64[] = [...BASE];` | Compiles |
| `[...p]` under `p != null`, `[...x]` with `x` a for...of binding of `T[]` | Compiles |
| `for (const j of [...queue]) { queue.push(s); }`, and `[...queue.clone()]` | Compiles; a statement temporary's loan dies by liveness |
| `[...a]` on a freestanding target with no allocator; reached from an `@interrupt` function | EN0820; EN0824 |
| User `clone` with `this`: it has no route to the source in safe code | Corpus trace; `Atomic` only |
| Deferred forms: `f(...a)`, `new C(...a)`, `console.log(...a)`, rest parameters, `(...a) => 1`, `{...o}`, `new Point { ...p }`, `const { a, ...r } = p`, `xs[...i]`, `for (const x of ...a)`, `@d([...a])`, `[a, ...r] = xs`, `const [a, ...r] = xs` | EN1013 at `...`, and no EN1010, EN1042 or EN0114 |
| `[a, r] = xs` and `const [a, r] = xs` with `xs: T[]` or `T[N]`; `const { a = 1 } = p`; `const [a = 1, b] = t` with a tuple `t` | EN1042 in phase 2, because only types tell an array from a tuple; EN1010 in phase 1 each |
| `EF-09`: `const { pair: [a, b] } = h; const { list: [c, d] } = h; const bad: string = 5;` with `pair: [i64, i64]` and `list: i64[]` | EN1042 at `[c, d]` and EN0401 at `5`, both in phase 2; the `pair` line compiles |
| `const [lo, hi] = minMax(&xs);`, `[a, b] = [b, a];`, `const { name: who, age } = &ada;` | Compiles |
| `WD-5`: `[a, b] = [b, a];` with `let a = "x" + "";` and `let b = "y" + "";`, and `const [p, q] = [1, 2];`, with no other context | Compiles and prints `y x 1 2` (twin agrees): a tuple pattern gives its right side an expected tuple type, so neither literal is an array |
| Nested: `const { name: who, pos: { x, y } } = &ada;`, `const { scores: [first, second] } = ada;`, `for (const [i, { name }] of &pairs)` | Compiles; prints `Ada 3 4`, `10 20`, `1 Ada` |
| `const { name, pos: { x } } = ada;` then `console.log(ada.name);`; `const { list: [a, b] } = h` with `list: T[]` | EN0601 (`` `ada.name` was moved into `name` ``); EN1042 |
| `[...a; 3]`; `[...0..n]`; `for (const i of a...b)` | EN0100 at `;`; at `..`; at `...`, with a fix-it to `..` |
| Lexer: `0..n`, `[...a]`, `[....5]`, `a...b`, `5...` | The tokens of SPEC 2; `.5` is EN0006 |
| `i64[].withCapacity(8)` beside `x[...` | The first is a `TypeMember`; the second is EN1013 |

**Inferred permissions.** The programs of the review of inferred permissions (SPEC 5) are permanent fixtures under their review names, with a `rustc` twin where the row names one. The loop-binding fixtures `f1`-`f6` exercise element writes through the loop variable, `h1`-`h8` the loop's hold, and `l1`-`l10` local functions among top-level statements.

| Fixture | Must give |
|---|---|
| `let x = 12; const y = &x; *y = 13;` | Compiles; `y` takes an exclusive loan, inferred from the write |
| The same, then `y = &z;` | EN0613: `y` is `const` |
| `let x = 12; const y = &x;`, then `x = 13;` before the last use of `y`; after it | EN0706; compiles |
| `let x = 12; const y = &x; console.log(x); *y = 13;` | EN0708: `x` is read before `y`'s last write |
| `const x = 12; let y = &x; *y = 13;`; the same with `y = &z;` before the write, `z` a `let` | EN0608 naming `x`, `cannot mutate immutable data`; compiles |
| `function read(x: &i64): void { console.log(*x); }` and `function update(x: &i64): void { *x = 42; }`, each called on `&n` while `const r = &n;` is still used | `read` compiles, with a shared loan; `update` is EN0706, with an exclusive loan |
| `const n = 1; bump(&n);` with a `bump` that writes; `fill(&data)` with `const data` and `function fill(let buf: &Slice<u8>): void { console.log(buf.length()); }` | EN0608 at the argument (twin E0596); EN0608, `` `fill` can change `buf`, but `data` is `const` `` |
| `h06`, the longest-word loop `let longest = &words[0]; for (const w of &words) { if (w.length() > longest.length()) { longest = w; } } console.log(longest);`; `b3`, the same with a later repoint of `longest` | Compile (twins accept); `h06` prints `bbb`; `b3` prints `bbb` then `xy` |
| The same loop over `["hello", "world", "programming"]`; over an empty array | Prints `programming`; panics out of bounds at `&words[0]` |
| `w06b`: `q = []; break;` in a loop over `&q`; `h4`: the same with a labelled `break` out of a nested loop, then `console.log(q)` | Compile (twins accept); `h4` prints `[]` |
| `h5`: `q = []; continue;` in a loop over `&q` | EN0706, `cannot reassign `q` during iteration` (twin E0506) |
| `a4`: `x` used after `q = [];` and before `break;` in a loop over `&q` | EN0706 (twin E0506) |
| `b1`: a reference repointed onto a `const` on one branch, then written through | EN0608 at the write, naming the `const` (twin E0596) |
| `b2`: a write through a `Box` reached from a `const` | EN0608 (twin E0596) |
| `b6`, `b7`: a function that writes its parameter and returns a read-only result; reading the result; writing through it | `b6` prints `7`; `b7` is EN0616 (twin E0594) |
| `b4`: a write through `players.last()` | Prints `[1, 1]` |
| `b5`: a receiver whose writable result follows it, evaluated before an argument that reads the same array | EN0708 (twin E0502) |
| `a5`: a write between two uses of a writing result | EN0707 at the write: the result is written through at its uses, so its loan is exclusive, and a write against a live exclusive loan is EN0707 (SPEC O11) (twin, not run for this row: E0499, as for `l8`) |
| `b11`: an element write, inside a loop over `&q`, through a writable result that follows `q` | EN0706 (twin E0502) |
| `b13`: a read through such a result, bound in the loop | Prints `1` |
| `l8`: a local function's hidden argument against an explicit `&count` in the same call | EN0707 (twin E0499) |
| `l9`: a local function that writes `q`, called inside a loop over `&q`; `l10`: the form that only reads | EN0706 (twin E0502); prints `7 7` |
| `b8`: a write from outside the class through `public peek(): readonly &i64`; the same through an unmarked `public peek(): &i64` | EN0616 (twin E0594), with a help that names the `readonly` in the return type of `peek`; compiles: a returned reference is as changeable as the object it came from |
| `b9`: a public method writing through `private peekInner(): readonly &i64 { return &this.kelvin; }`; the same with no `readonly` | EN0616, even inside the class; prints `299` |
| `b10`: a write through `public top(): &i64` | Prints `9` |
| `a7`, `a10`, `a12`: element reads inside a hold: a plain read, an `Atomic` read, a reader `fn` value | Compile; print `9`, `1` and `1` |
| `private unsafe let count: i64 = 0;` and a `Logger` with `public const log(const m: &string): void { unsafe { count += 1; } console.log(m); }`, called as `const l = new Logger(); l.log("a");`; the same without the `const` before `log` | Compiles; EN0614, `help: if `log` doesn't change the logger, mark it `const`` |
| `WD-2`: the same `Logger` with `public const log(m: &string)`, called as `l.log("a")`; with `GREETING`, a top-level `private const GREETING: &string = "hi";`, as the argument | EN0616 at `"a"`: in a body containing `unsafe`, `m` counts as written (N12), and a string literal is read-only; EN0608 at the argument (a `const GREETING: string`, or one with no type, does not type-check, since a constant can't hold a `string`) |
| A method marked `const` whose body contains `unsafe` and passes `this` to a helper that contains `unsafe` and is not marked `const` | EN0615: the check is transitive |
| An `@interrupt` handler whose body contains `unsafe` and only reads its frame; one that writes its frame | Compiles, with no `const`, because role parameters are read-only; EN0615 |
| `f1`-`f6`, loop bindings, among them `let nums: i64[] = [1, 2]; for (const n of &nums) { *n *= 2; }`, `bump(n)` with a `bump` that writes, `item.count += 1` over a `let` source, the same over a `const` source, `nums.push(9)` in the body | `[2, 4]`; `[2, 3]`; compiles; EN0608 at the source; EN0706, `cannot mutate `nums` while iterating over it` |
| `h1`-`h8`, hold slots: loans on the source path and on each reference slot or reference field it goes through, live while a next head of the same run is reachable; among them `h8`, `this.add(x)` in a loop over `&this.items` where `add` changes another field | EN0706 where a next head is reachable after the change, compiles where it is not; `h8` is EN0706 (twin E0502) |
| `l1`-`l10`, local functions, among them `let count = 0; function inc(): void { count += 1; } inc(); console.log(count); inc();`; `const r = &count; inc(); console.log(r);`; `for (const x of &q) { reset(); }` with a `reset` that changes `q`; `inc` writing a `const count`; `function report(): void { console.log(count); } let count = 0;`; `report(); let count = 0; function report(): void { console.log(count); }` | Compiles; EN0706, as `inc(&count)` would be; EN0706; EN0608 at the write; EN0313; EN0618 |

**Corpus programs for the same rules,** all cells unless stated, each with `//! allocs: balanced` where it terminates: an index loop that grows its array (`1 2 3 10 20 30`) and a `pop()` worklist (`3 2 1 0`), the forms that replace growth inside `for...of`; `q = []; break;` and `q.push(1); break;` in a loop over `&q`, and assignment after the loop; looping over `q.clone()` or `[...q]` while assigning `q` (`1 2 3`); element writes through the loop variable (`[2, 4]`); an element kept from an earlier turn and written after the loop (`[1, 5, 3, 0]`); zero-sized elements with `break`; elements with destructors under `ds.pop()` and `ds.truncate(0)` in an index loop and under `*x = v` in a `for...of`, with a destructor trace; spread order; deep clone independence; `break` over `[...q]`; `a = [...a]`; a panic mid-clone, whose partial output leaks and is exempt from the balance check (D6). On `//! cells: vm` only: `out of memory` at `--max-memory`, from an index loop that pushes every turn. Accepted path-source programs also run under `--oracle`, and any fault is a soundness bug.

**The attack review of the applied design.** The programs of the attack review of 2026-11-17 are permanent fixtures under their finding names: `D20-core-1` to `D20-core-7`, `D20E-01` to `D20E-11`, `INF-01` to `INF-13`, `MR-1` to `MR-9`, `F1` to `F14`, `EF-01` to `EF-11` and `WD-1` to `WD-6`. A second word, as in `D20-core-1 both`, names another program of the same finding, and `MR-lead` is the `Sink` program that the modules review passed to the borrowing-class review. The examples of the owner's answers to the review's open questions 12 to 26 are permanent fixtures too, named `OQ` with the question's number and a word, as in `OQ18-retag`, and each sits beside the finding it settles or the row it extends. Each outcome was traced by hand through the fixed rules, and a row names the `rustc` twin's result, by the translation of section 13, where one was run. Rows that extend an existing row sit beside it in the tables above (`INF-11`, `INF-12`, `WD-2`, `WD-4`, `WD-5`, `WD-6`, `EF-09`, `F14`, `OQ12-fn`, `OQ22-first`, `OQ24-nested`), the fmt programs `EF-05` and `EF-11` are in section 17, `F6`, `MR-7`, `OQ16-grow` and `OQ16-recheck` are interface-file meta-tests below, and `OQ15-reload`, `OQ16-dev`, `OQ17-reload` and `OQ19-reload` are reload scenarios. Accepted programs also run under `--oracle`, which keeps tags in the reference fields of borrowing values (`DEV_RUNTIME.md` section 3), and any fault is a soundness bug.

Borrowing classes and returned references:

| Fixture | Must give |
|---|---|
| `D20-core-1`: `private class Grower { private v: &i64[]; ... }` with `public setV(v: &i64[]): void { this.v = v; }` and `public grow(): void { this.v.push(4); }`; `g.setV(&data); const e = &data[0]; g.grow(); console.log(*e);` | EN0708 at `&data[0]`: `Grower` writes through its reference field, so the stored `&data` is exclusive (twin E0502; E0596 in `grow` with a shared `&'a` field) |
| `D20-core-1 both`: a `Keeper` whose `grow()` pushes through its field, and `function both(k: &Keeper, a: &i64[]): &i64[] { k.set(a); return a; }` | EN0708 at `return a`: the exclusive reborrow that `set` stored in `k` is live until the return (twin E0499) |
| `D20-core-1 literal`: `a.setS("hi")` on a class whose `add()` runs `*this.s += "!"`; the same with a `const` local as the argument | EN0616 at `"hi"` (twin E0596); EN0608 at the argument |
| `D20E-05`: `const first = &xs[0];` used after `{ const p = new Pusher(&xs); console.log(p.size()); }`, where `Pusher`'s destructor pushes 8 times through its field; the same with no destructor and `p.add(2)`, where `add` pushes; a `Bumper` whose destructor runs `*this.r += 1`, built over `const n: i64 = 5` | EN0706 at `&xs` in both forms, because a destructor's write counts as a method's does (twin E0502); EN0608 at `&n` |
| `INF-03`: `View` with `public cur(): &Bag { return this.r; }`; `let v = new View(&a); const q = &a.items[0];`, then `v.cur().items.push(i)` in a loop and `console.log(q)` | EN0708 at `&a.items[0]`: a method that returns a reference field as a writable result writes through it (twin E0502; E0308 in `cur` with a shared field) |
| `WD-1`: `Cursor` whose `public next(): &string \| null` returns `this.xs.get(this.i)`; `c.setXs(&data); const part = data[0].slice(0, 1); const t = c.next()!; *t = "x" + "y"; console.log(part);`; the same with `new Cursor(&data)` in place of `setXs` | EN0708 at `data[0].slice(0, 1)` both ways (twin E0502) |
| `MR-lead`: `Sink` whose `add(s: &string)` runs `*this.out += s`; `let msg: string = "a"; const r = &msg; let sink = new Sink(&msg); sink.add("b"); console.log(r);`; `new Sink("literal")` | EN0706 at `&msg` (twin E0502); EN0616 at the literal |
| `D20-core-2`: `Holder` with `public first(): &i64 { return &this.v[0]; }` and `public reset(): void { *this.v = [9, 9]; }`; `const e = h.first(); h.reset(); console.log(*e);` | EN0706 at `h.reset()`: a class that writes through its reference fields gives no result that holds only their loans, so `e` holds the loan on `h` (twin E0502 with the result tied to `&self`; a `&'a` result from a `&'a mut` field fails in `first`) |

| `SLB-01-value`: `let s: string = "a".toString(); console.log(s += "!"); console.log(s);`, then `console.log((s += "?"));`; `const t = (s += "?");`; `a = b = "xx".toString();` with `let a: string = "".toString();` and `let b: string = "".toString();`; `x = y = 0; console.log(x, y);` with `let x: i64 = 1;` and `let y: i64 = 2;`; `let s = "ab".toString(); both(&(s += "c"), &s);` with `function both(a: &string, b: &string): void { const p = b.slice(0, 1); *a += "xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx"; console.log(p); }`; `const r = head(&(s += "c")); s = "z".toString(); console.log(r);` with `function head(x: &string): &string { return x.slice(0, 1); }`; `console.log(s += "!")` over that `s` | Prints `a!`, `a!` and `a!?`: the value of an assignment to a place that is not Copy is the target place itself, read after the store, so `s` stays its only owner, and a use that does not keep it, as a `console.log` argument, a comparison, a template hole, an operator or a `&` argument, borrows it as the same use with `s` written by name would, shared here, until that use's temporaries end, the end of the statement here (SPEC D2); EN0621 at `(s += "?")`, `an assignment's value can't be kept`, labelled `` `s += "?"` is still owned by `s` ``, with `` help: assign first, then copy: `s += "?"; const t = s.clone();` ``, because a use that would keep the value, a binding initialiser, the right side of another assignment, a by-value argument, a `return`, an element or field of a literal or a capture by value, would make a second owner of `s`; EN0621 at `b = "xx".toString()`, the right side of `a = ...`, with `` help: assign one at a time: `b = "xx".toString(); a = b.clone();` ``; prints `0 0`: a Copy place keeps its copy, so a chain of assignments sets both (Node prints `a!`, then `a!? a!? xx xx` for `console.log(s, t, a, b)`, and `0 0`; Rust prints `"a?" ()` for `let t = (s += "?");` and gives E0308 `` expected `String`, found `()` `` for `a = b = s2;`, and `` expected `i64`, found `()` `` for `x = y = 0;`; Swift gives `cannot assign value of type '()' to type 'String'` for `a = b = s2`, and warns `constant 't' inferred to have type '()'` and prints `() a!` for the kept value; g++, where the value is a reference to `s`, prints `a!? a!? a!? xx xx`, and C prints `0 0`); EN0708 at `&s`: `&(s += "c")` borrows `s` as `&s` would for `both`'s writing `a`, exclusive, so the second argument's shared borrow conflicts, where a reading that made every such borrow shared and short let `*a += ...` reallocate what `p` reads (twin E0502 `` cannot borrow `s` as immutable because it is also borrowed as mutable ``); EN0706 at `s = "z".toString()`, since `r` keeps the loan that `&(s += "c")` took, as `head(&s)` would (twin E0506 `` cannot assign to `s` because it is borrowed ``); prints `ab!`, unchanged |
| `SLB-01-readline`: `` private function next(i: i64): string \| null { return i < 2 ? `line${i}` : null; } `` and `let line: string \| null = null; let i: i64 = 0; while ((line = next(i++)) != null) { console.log(line.length(), line.toUpperCase()); }` | Compiles and prints `5 LINE0` then `5 LINE1`: the `!= null` test only reads the assignment's value, `line` itself after the store, and narrows `line` in the loop body by N2, as `tsc` does (`tsc` exits 0 on the same loop, and Node prints `5 LINE0` and `5 LINE1`) |
| `INF-09`: `let keep: &i64 \| null = null; for (const x of &q) { if (x > 2) { keep = x; } } if (keep != null) { *keep = 0; } console.log(q);` over `q = [1, 5, 3, 7]`; the same loop in `findLast(list: &Slice<i64>): &i64 \| null` | Compiles and prints `[1, 5, 3, 0]` both ways: every turn of a `for...of` binds at the loop's one bind site, whose loans the loop's own head and bind skip, so they skip the binds of its earlier turns, and compared with one another the binds of two turns of one run are disjoint too, while those of two runs overlap (`S2-25-pair`). Both twins compile and print `[1, 5, 3, 0]`, the second over `list.iter_mut()` by the translation of section 13, where the saved-reference form that translation once used is E0499 and E0502 (`TK-02`) |
| `OQ26-keys`: the inline `INF-09` loop, then `for (const y of &q) { console.log(y); }` before the write through `keep`; the same with `console.log(q[0]);` in place of the second loop; the same with `let n = 0; while (n < 2) { for (const x of &q) { keep = x; } n += 1; }` in place of the loop | EN0708 at the second loop's `&q`; EN0708 at `q[0]` (twin E0502 each): the keys of different loops, and of a loop and any other access to `q`, still overlap as `[*]` does; EN0707 at the inner loop's `&q`, where its second run takes its hold at the pre-header, checked against the binding kept from the first run (twin E0499) |

Interfaces, classes and layout:

| Fixture | Must give |
|---|---|
| `D20E-01`: `function wrap(s: Shower): Box<Shower> { return new Box(s); }` and `return wrap(new View(&tmp));`, with `View` holding `private s: &string` | EN0701 at `new View(&tmp)`: a `Shower` can't hold a `View`, because `View` holds a reference (twin E0515) |
| `F1`: `keep(new Echo(&text))` with `function keep(s: Speaker): Box<Speaker>`; `const b: Box<Speaker> = new Box(new Echo(&text));`; `introduce(&echo)` with `introduce(s: &Speaker)`; each with a class that holds no reference | EN0701 each, at the argument, at `Box<Echo>` and at `&echo` (twin E0310, then E0597 and E0506 with a `'static` bound); compiles |
| `F2`: `const pet: Box<Animal> = new Box(new Dog("Rex"));` dropped without `unbox`; `pet.unbox()`, `*a = new Animal("x")`, `mem.replace(a, ...)`, `mem.swap(a, ...)` and `pet.clone()`, with `a: &Animal` at a `Dog`; a Copy read-through, `const c: P = *r;`, with `public class P implements Copy { public x: i64; }`, `public class Q extends P { public y: i64; }` and `r: &P` at a `Q`, since this `Animal` holds a string and is not Copy (`WD2-5`) | The block is freed with `Dog`'s size, read from its method table, so the VM reports no `Bad free`; each of the rest compares the object's method table with `Animal`'s and panics with a message that names the operation and what holds the object: `pet.unbox()` with `` `unbox` expected an `Animal`, but the box holds a `Dog` ``, `pet.clone()` with `` `clone` expected an `Animal`, but the box holds a `Dog` ``, `mem.replace(a, ...)` with `` `mem.replace` expected an `Animal`, but the reference points at a `Dog` ``, `mem.swap(a, ...)` with `` `mem.swap` expected an `Animal`, but the reference points at a `Dog` ``, `*a = new Animal("x")` with `` assignment expected an `Animal`, but the reference points at a `Dog` ``, and the Copy read-through with `` copy expected a `P`, but the reference points at a `Q` `` (twin E0277) |
| `OQ15-unbox`: `const pet: Box<Animal> = new Box(new Dog("Rex")); console.log(pet.speak()); let a = pet.unbox();`; the same over `new Box(new Animal("Generic"))`, then `console.log(a.speak())` | Prints `Rex barks`, then panics `` `unbox` expected an `Animal`, but the box holds a `Dog` ``: a call through the box needs no check; prints `Generic makes a sound` twice, since an exact `Animal` passes the check (twin E0277 on `*pet`) |
| `F5`: `function keepName(x: { name: string }): { name: string } { return x; }` given a `User` with a destructor; `dispose(x)` in such a function | EN0440 at `return x;`, `` `x` may have more fields than `{ name: string }` ``, with the help to build `{ name: x.name }` (twin E0308); compiles, and `User`'s destructor runs |
| `F7`: `introduce(rexRef)` with `introduce(s: &Speaker)` and `rexRef: &Animal` at a `Dog`; the same through `<T extends Speaker>` with `T := Animal`; `for (const s of &all) { introduce(s); }` over a `Box<Speaker>[]`, and `&*b` or `b.unbox()` on a `Box<Speaker>` | Prints `Rex barks` both ways: in a copy for a class that can be extended, interface and bound member calls go through the object's table. The loop and `introduce(&*b)` compile: an `&Speaker` taken from a box is two words, the object and its method table, and reaches the one extra copy of `introduce` whose member calls go through that table; `b.unbox()` is EN1006, because a `Speaker` by value outside a parameter has no known size (twin E0277; compiles with `?Sized`) |
| `OQ17-introduce`: `const all: Box<Speaker>[] = [new Box(new Dog {}), new Box(new Robot {})]; for (const s of &all) { introduce(s); }` with `private function introduce(s: &Speaker): void { console.log(s.speak()); }`, then `introduce(&rex)` with `const rex = new Dog {};` | Prints `woof`, `beep`, then `woof`: the boxed values reach the copy of `introduce` for a two-word `&Speaker`, made only because such a value reaches it, and `&rex` reaches the copy for `Dog` (twin with `?Sized` prints `woof`, `beep`, `woof`) |
| `F8`: `const pets: Box<Animal>[] = [new Box(new Animal("Generic")), new Box(new Dog("Fido"))];` with `console.log(p.speak())` over `&pets` | Compiles and prints `Generic makes a sound` then `Fido barks` (twin agrees): the expected type is only a hint, so where `new Dog("Fido")` cannot become an `Animal` the call is typed from its arguments, and its `Box<Dog>` result converts by C21. `Ok(x)` with `x: i64` at `Result<i64 \| null>` still takes `T` from the expected type and compiles |
| `F9`: `private unsafe let stats: Stats;` with a `Stats` that is not `final`; the same under `@at(...)`; with `public final class Stats` | EN0802 each, with the help to write `public final class Stats` or give it a value, `= new Stats {}`; compiles |
| `F11`: `public class A { public a: i64; public b: u8; }` and `public class D extends A { public c: u8; }` on `x86_64` | `offsetOf<D>("c")` is 24 and `sizeOf<D>()` is 32: a subclass never reuses its base's tail padding |
| `F13`: `console.log({ name: "Ada", age: 36 });` and `const c = { age: 36, name: "Ada" }; console.log(c);` | Both print `{ age: 36, name: 'Ada' }` on every cell: fields print in name byte order, through `Display`, and the string inside prints in single quotes (`EP-09`) |
| `OQ19-header`: `console.log(h)`, ``console.log(`header: ${h}`)`` and `console.log(h.toString())` with `const h = new Header { len: 3, flags: 0 };`, `public class Header { public len: u16; public flags: u8; }` and no `fmt`; the same with `flags` private and set by a constructor; `console.log(hs.join(", "))` with `const hs = [new Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }, new Header { len: 4, flags: 1 }];`; the first three with a `fmt` on `Header` | Prints `Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }`, `header: Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }` and `Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }`, and the same three with the private field: every class satisfies `Display`, and one with no `fmt` prints field by field, in declaration order; prints `Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }, Header { len: 4, flags: 1 }`; prints what its `fmt` writes, three times |
| `OQ19-values`: `console.log(new Box(5))`; `console.log(u)` with `public class User { public name: string; public age: i64; }` and `const u = new User { name: "Ada", age: 36 };`; `console.log(s)` with `public class Slot { public v: i64 \| null; }` and `const s = new Slot { v: null };`; `const t: [i64, i64] = [1, 5]; console.log(t);`; `console.log(q)` and `console.log(r)` with `public class Shape { public color: string; }`, `public class Square extends Shape { public side: i64; }`, `const q = new Square { color: "red", side: 2 };` and `const r: &Shape = &q;`; `` console.log(`${p}`) `` with `const p = &u;`, and `console.log(label(&u))` with `` function label<T>(x: &T): string { return `${x}`; } `` | Prints `5`; `User { name: 'Ada', age: 36 }`, the string in single quotes (`EP-09`); `Slot { v: null }`; `[1, 5]`; `Square { color: 'red', side: 2 }` twice, the base class's fields first, and the `Square` through the `&Shape`; `User { name: 'Ada', age: 36 }` twice: a reference prints what it points to, a type parameter with no bound meets `Display`, and a string inside the value a hole prints is quoted, as inside any other value |
| `OQ19-address`: `console.log(f)` with `const f: fn(x: i64) => i64 = double;` and `private function double(x: i64): i64 { return x * 2; }`; `console.log(g)` with `const g = () => 1;`; `console.log(0xB8000 as *u16)` | Compiles, and each prints a hex number, `0x` and lowercase digits with no padding: the address of `double`'s code, the address of the arrow's code, and the pointer's address, `0xb8000`, which is the same on every cell, VM cells included, because making a pointer from an integer never faults (`MD-7`). The first two differ by cell, and on a VM cell they are VM addresses, so the program declares `//! differs: address` (section 12) |
| `OQ19-glue`: `console.log(h)` with the `Header` of `OQ19-header` and no `fmt`, in two functions; `console.log(e)` with `const e = DiskError.BadSector { lba: 42 };` | Prints `Header { len: 3, flags: 0 }` twice and `DiskError.BadSector { lba: 42 }`, with an allocation count of 0 on every cell: the `DisplayGlue` that mono makes once for `Header` and once for `DiskError` writes piece by piece into the `Formatter` and never allocates. This is the `DisplayGlue` shim's test |
| `MR-9`: `private const SINK_BYTES = sizeOf<Sink>();` with a borrowing `Sink`, used as `u8[SINK_BYTES]` in a method of `Sink`; `sizeOf<Sink>()` inside a body | EN0412, with a note that the layout depends on what the class's methods do with its references; compiles, folded at mono (the twin fixes the form in the type and prints `16 8`) |
| `MR-1`: `run(shout, &msg); run(show, &msg);` over `let msg: string = "hi"`, with `function run(f: fn(let s: &string) => void, s: &string): void`, a `shout` that runs `*s += "!"` and a `show` that only reads; `emit(&k, &msg)` where `Quiet.put` prints `s.length()` and overrides `Sink.put(let s: &string)` | Compiles and prints `hi!` then `3`: strings keep one type, and `show` and `Quiet.put`, whose `&string` only reads and so is a pointer and a length, are each reached through an adapter the compiler emits, which loads the pointer and length from the whole string the writing row passes, so each gets the string the caller passed (twin E0308; with an explicit adapter it prints `hi!`). Arrays raise no such case, because a function that sets elements takes `&Slice<T>` and one that pushes takes `&T[]`, so the type fixes the form |
| `MR-2`: with `let buf: u8[4]`, `fill(&buf)` where `fill(xs: &Slice<u8>)` sets `xs[0]`; `addOne(&buf)` where `addOne(xs: &u8[])` pushes; `reset(&buf)` where `reset(xs: &u8[])` calls `mem.replace(xs, [9])` | Compiles; EN0401, `a fixed array can't grow`, whose help gives both ways, `xs: &Slice<u8>` if `addOne` only reads or sets elements, or a `u8[]` for `buf` to let it grow (`SL-10`); EN0401 the same way (twin: `fill` accepted, `add_one` E0308, `reset` E0277) |
| `OQ13-slice`: `let sector: u8[8] = [0; 8]; let list: u8[] = [1, 2, 3]; fill(sector.slice(4, 8), 7); fill(&list, 5); addOne(&list); console.log(sector, list, checksum(&sector));` with `fill(xs: &Slice<u8>, v: u8)`, `addOne(xs: &u8[])` and `checksum(data: &Slice<u8>): u32`; then `addOne(&sector)`, `addOne(list.slice(0, 2))` and `sector.slice(0, 4).push(1)`; a field `public s: Slice<u8>;` | Prints `[0, 0, 0, 0, 7, 7, 7, 7] [5, 5, 5, 1] 28`: a part from `slice`, a fixed array and a whole growable array all convert to `&Slice<u8>`, and writing through a part changes the original; EN0401, `a fixed array can't grow`, whose help gives both ways (`SL-10`); EN0401, `a part of an array can't grow`, with `help: pass the whole array: addOne(&list)`, since the argument is a `slice` call on a whole array; EN0303, because a `Slice` has no `push`; EN0401, `` `Slice<u8>` is a view, so it exists only behind `&` `` (twin prints the same line, and is E0308 for a fixed array where `&mut Vec<u8>` is expected) |
| `MR-3`: `const r = longest(&s, &t); *r += "!";` with SPEC 5.4's `longest`; `longer(&x, &y).push(9);` with `function longer(a: &i64[], b: &i64[]): &i64[]`, `x = [1, 2]` and `y = [3]` | EN0616, `` `longest` returns a string that may be a part, so it can't grow ``, with `` help: change `s` directly: `s += "!";` ``; compiles, and `console.log(x)` prints `[1, 2, 9]`, because a `&i64[]` is always a whole array (twins print `abc!` with `&mut String`, are E0368 with `&mut str`, and print `[1, 2, 9]` with `&mut Vec<i64>`) |
| `OQ14-grow`: `*grown(&s) += "!";` with `function grown(s: &string): &string { *s += "?"; return s; }` and `let s: string = "abc"`; `const r = s.slice(0, 2); *r += "!";` | Compiles, and `console.log(s)` prints `abc?!`: a result from a parameter the function grows is a whole string (twin prints `abc?!`); EN0616, `` a part of a string can't grow; copy it with `.toString()` `` |

Local functions, decorators and marks:

| Fixture | Must give |
|---|---|
| `MR-4`: `function relay(): void { grow(); }` beside `function grow(): void { for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) { q.push(i); } }`, then `const f: fn() => void = relay;`; `grow()` called from a class method | EN0803 at `relay`, whose hidden arguments include those of `grow`; EN0313 at the call in the method (twin E0502 with the argument threaded through, E0133 through a static) |
| `MR-5`: `@devMigrate function migratePlayer(old: { name: string, hp: i32 }): Player` among the top-level statements, pushing to a top-level `log`; the same with `@panicHandler`; `@mustUse` on such a function, and `@devMigrate` on one that uses no top-level variable | EN0806 at each decorator: a runtime calls the function, and nothing supplies its hidden arguments (twin E0502, E0596 and E0597 for a panic hook that captures); compiles |
| `INF-07`: `public label(): &string { return "animal"; }` in a base class, overridden by `public override label(): &string { return &this.name; }`; `introduce(&animal)` and `introduce(&rex)` with an `introduce(a: &Animal)` that prints `a.label()` | Compiles and prints `animal` then `Rex`: an override may return into `this` as well as into its base body's sources (twin compiles and prints `animal` then `Rex`) |
| `OQ18-retag`: `Animal` with `public label(): &string { return "animal"; }` and `public rename(name: string): void { this.name = name; }`, a `Dog` whose `label` override returns `&this.name`, and `private function retag(a: &Animal): void { const tag = a.label(); a.rename("Max"); console.log(tag); }` called as `retag(&rex)` on `let rex = new Dog("Rex")`; the same with `const tag = a.label().toString();` | EN0706 at `a.rename("Max")`, `` `a` can't change while `tag` points into it ``, with `help: keep a copy: const tag = a.label().toString();`: a call through a base reference to a class that isn't `final` locks the object (twin E0502); compiles and prints `Rex` (twin with `.to_string()` prints `Rex`) |
| `OQ18-choose`: `Picker` with `protected favorite: string`, `public pick(a: &string, b: &string): &string { return a.length() > b.length() ? a : b; }` and `public reset(): void { this.favorite = ""; }`, with `private function choose(p: &Picker, x: &string, y: &string): void { const best = p.pick(x, y); p.reset(); console.log(best); }`; the same with `public final class Picker`; the first `Picker` again, with `p` a `let` local of `choose` that holds a new `Picker`, in place of the parameter; `const best = this.pick(x, y); this.reset(); console.log(best);` in a method of that `Picker` with `x: &string` and `y: &string` parameters | EN0706 at `p.reset()`, `` `p` can't change while `best` points into it ``, with `help: keep a copy: const best = p.pick(x, y).toString();`: a call through the method table locks the object as well as `x` and `y`; compiles, because the methods of a `final` class add nothing to their body's sources, so `best` locks only `x` and `y`; compiles, because a local whose type is the class holds exactly a `Picker`, so the call uses `Picker.pick`'s own sources; EN0706 at `this.reset()`, because `this` may be a subclass |
| `OQ18-overrides`: subclasses of that `Picker` whose `pick` overrides return `&this.favorite` (`Loyal`), `b` (`Lazy`) and `"none"` (`Silent`); an override of `public abstract label(): &string;` that returns `&this.name`; `const r = Pair.first(&a, &b); b.push(1); console.log(r);` with a `static` `first` that returns `a` | Compile each: an override may return into `this` or into any source of its base body, an abstract base keeps the signature default, `this`, and a `static` method adds nothing to its body's sources |
| `OQ18-strict`: `Labeler` with `public label(name: &string, fallback: &string): &string { return name; }`, and `Strict extends Labeler` overriding it with `return fallback;` | EN0714 at the override's `return`, `` `Strict.label` returns a reference into `fallback`, but `Labeler.label` returns only into `name` or `this` ``, with `` help: through a `&Labeler`, callers only expect `name` or the object `` |
| `INF-06`, `MR-6`: `fill(sector.slice(4, 8), 7)` with `sector: u8[8]` and a `fill(part: &Slice<u8>, v: u8)` that sets every element; `data.slice(0, 2).length()` and `data.last()` on a `const data`; `list.push(4)` while `const head = list.slice(0, 2);` is used later | Writes into `sector`; compiles; compiles; EN0706, `` `list` can't change while `head` points into it ``. The library declares `public const slice(a, b): &Slice<T>`, whose body contains `unsafe` and only reads, and `public get(i): &T \| null` and `public last(): &T \| null`, whose results are as changeable as the array (twin: `at` beside `at_mut` prints `1 2` and `[1, 2, 7, 4]`; `at(&mut self)` alone is E0499) |
| `INF-08`, `WD-3`: `Tokens implements TokenReader`, with `let next(): readonly &string \| null;` in the interface and a `next` that advances `pos` and returns `this.text.slice(start, this.pos)`, with no marks in the class, over `"let x = 42"`, each token printed before the next call; the same with no `readonly` in the interface; `firstTwo<T extends TokenReader>(t: &T)`, and a function taking `t: &TokenReader`, each keeping `a` across a second `t.next()` (`const a = t.next(); const b = t.next(); console.log(a, b);`) | Compiles and prints `let x = 42`; EN0615 at the class, with the help to write `let next(): readonly &string \| null;` in `TokenReader`; EN0707 at the second `t.next()` in both, as the second attack pass settled (`RR-06`), because a member with no body returns from `this` (SPEC 5.4) and changes it, with `help: keep a copy: const a = t.next()?.toString()` and the help to use the concrete class (twins compile and print `let x = 42`; the twin of the two-token functions is E0499 `` cannot borrow `*t` as mutable more than once at a time ``, and only a borrowing reader with a named lifetime prints `Some("let") Some("x")`, which `RR-06` keeps from an interface) |
| `MR-8`: `public update(): void {}` in `Entity`, overridden in `Player` by a body that runs `this.x += 1`; the same with `public let update(): void {}` in `Entity`, then `p.update(); console.log(p.x);` | EN0615 at `this.x += 1` in `Player`'s override, its first write of the object (SPEC G18), because `Entity.update` reads the object; compiles and prints `1`: `let` before the name lets overrides change the object although the base body doesn't (twin E0053; with `&mut self` in the base it prints `1`) |
| `OQ12-count`: a `Counter` with `public count(): readonly &isize { return &this.n; }`; `console.log(*c.count())` and `*c.count() = 5;` on a `let c` | Compiles; EN0616 at the write, `` cannot change the object returned by `count` ``, with a help that names the `readonly` in the return type of `count` |
| `OQ12-cost`: a `Stack` with `private items: i64[] = [1, 2];`, `public all(): &i64[] { return &this.items; }`, `public view(): readonly &i64[] { return &this.items; }` and `public size(): isize { return this.items.length(); }`; `let s = new Stack(); s.all().push(99); console.log(s.size());`; `s.view().push(99);` | Prints `3`, as TypeScript does: a method that leaves out `readonly` lets callers change what it returns; EN0614, with `` help: to allow this, remove `readonly` from the return type of `view` `` (`WD2-3`; TypeScript gives TS2339) |

Errors and `Result`:

| Fixture | Must give |
|---|---|
| `EF-02`: `return [lo, hi];` in `Result<[i64, i64]>`, `return null;` in `Result<isize \| null>`, `return [];` in `Result<i64[]>`, `return 200;` in `Result<u8>`, `return { x: 1, y: 2 };` in `Result<Point>` | Compiles, each a success: an operand whose type comes from its context is checked at `T`, then wrapped |
| `EF-10`: `if (dryRun) { return; }` in a function returning `Result<void>`, and the same in a test arrow `(): Result<void> => { ... }` | Compiles: `return;` there means `return Ok();` |
| `V-ME-09-arrow`: `const check = (s: &string) => { if (s.isEmpty()) { return Err("empty"); } return s.length(); };`; `const check2 = (s: &string) => { if (!s.isEmpty()) { return s.trim().toString(); } return Err("empty"); };`; the first written `(s: &string): Result<isize> => { ... }` | EN0401 each, the first with `` help: annotate the result, `(s: &string): Result<isize> => ...`, or write `Ok(s.length())` ``: a return type inferred from the body is never a site where C19 wraps, so with no expected type `Err(e)` takes its error type from its operand, `Failure` for a text, and a fresh success type, as `Ok` does, and the returns unify unwrapped, whatever their order (twin E0308 `` mismatched types ``, `` expected `Result<_, &str>`, found `usize` ``); compiles |
| `EF-03`: `Err("left")` in `Result<i64, Direction>` with `private type Direction = "left" \| "right";`; `Err("up")` there; `Err("no")` in `Result<i64, AgeError>`; `return Token.End;` in `Result<Token>`, and `Err(AgeError.Negative)` in `Result<i64>` | Compiles, an error holding `Direction`'s `"left"`; EN0401; EN0401, because a text makes a `Failure` only when the error type is `Failure`; compiles, a success, because EN0455 fires only where `Err` would also take the value as it is, a text where the error type is `Failure` or a string-literal union, or a value whose own type is the error type, while `Err` would turn a `Token` into its text; compiles, an error holding the text `negative age` |
| `OQ20-age`: a `parseAge` returning `Result<i64>` with `if (n == null) { return Err(AgeError.NotANumber); }` and `NotANumber = "not a number"` in `AgeError`, called as `show(parseAge("x"))` with a `show` that prints `` `caught: ${r.error}` `` for an error; `return Token.End;` in `Result<Token, Token>`; `return "file missing";` in `Result<string>`; `Err(DiskError.Timeout)` in `Result<i64, AgeError>` | Prints `caught: not a number`: `Err(x)` at `Result<T>` turns an error of another type into its text, as `?` does; EN0455, because the value's own type is the declared error type; EN0455; EN0401, because at a named error type `Err` needs that type |
| `S2-33-err`: `public function checkName(name: &string): Result<string>` that builds `msg` in a `let msg: string`, which holds `"name is empty"` for an empty name, and runs `if (!msg.isEmpty()) { return Err(msg); }` before `return Ok(name.toUpperCase());`, called as `show(checkName(""))` with a `show` that prints `` `caught: ${r.error}` `` for an error; `private function fail(why: &string): Result<u8> { return Err(why); }`, on a hosted target and on a freestanding one with no `@globalAllocator`; `return msg;` in `checkName`; `return name.toUpperCase();` there | Prints `caught: name is empty`: where the error type is `Failure`, `Err` takes any `string` or `&string` expression, and `Err(msg)` moves the `string` into the `Failure` with no copy (Rust's `return Err(msg)` prints `Err("name is empty")`, and Node's `throw new Error(msg)` prints `error: name is empty`); compiles, copying the text `why` points at; EN0820 at `Err(why)`, since the copy needs memory; EN0455, `` `msg` could be a success or an error here ``, with `` help: write `Ok(msg)` or `Err(msg)` ``, because `Err` would take `msg` as it is (twin E0308 `` expected `Result<String, String>`, found `String` ``, with the help `Ok(msg)` or `Err(msg)`); EN0455 the same way, since `Err` would take that `string` too |
| `V-ME-06-err`: a `public class User` with `public constructor(private name: string) {}` and `public check(): Result<isize> { if (this.name.isEmpty()) { return Err(this.name); } return this.name.length(); }`; `public function firstErr(names: &Slice<string>): Result<i64> { return Err(names[0]); }`; `let last: string = "none".toString(); const fail = (): Result<i64> => Err(last);`; `public label(): Result<string> { return this.name; }` in `User`; `return Err(&this.name);` in `check` | EN0603 at `this.name`, with `` help: copy the text: `Err(&this.name)` `` and the help `this.name.clone()`: `Err` copies only an operand of type `&string`, and moves a `string`, so a `string` place that no move may leave follows the move rules, as `const n = this.name;` does (twin E0507 `` cannot move out of `self.name` which is behind a shared reference `` with `help: consider cloning the value if the performance cost is acceptable`); EN0604 at `names[0]`, with the same helps (twin E0507 `` cannot move out of index of `Vec<String>` `` with `` help: you can `clone` the value and consume it, but this might not be your desired behavior ``); EN0617 at `last`, with the same helps; EN0455, with `` help: write `Ok(this.name.clone())` or `Err(&this.name)` ``, never an `Ok(this.name)` that would be EN0603; compiles, copying the text |
| `EF-01`: `` function show<E extends Error>(e: &E): string { return `${e}`; } ``; `lift(parse(b))` into `Result<i64>` with `PacketError.Truncated { got: u8[] }`, printed with `${r.error}` for `got: [1, 2, 3]` | Compiles, because `Error` extends `Display`; compiles and prints `PacketError.Truncated { got: [1, 2, 3] }`: a case's text holds its fields, and an array prints as `console.log` prints it (twin E0277) |
| `OQ19-packet`: `PacketError.BadHeader { header: new Header { len: 3, flags: 0 } }`, with the `Header` of `OQ19-header`, and `DiskError.BadSector { lba: 42 }`, each through `?` into `Result<u8>` and printed with `${r.error}`; the first again with a `fmt` on `Header` | Prints `PacketError.BadHeader { header: Header { len: 3, flags: 0 } }` and `DiskError.BadSector { lba: 42 }`: no enum is refused as an error type; the header's part is what its `fmt` writes. Each conversion calls `failure_from_display` once, and `allocs: balanced` holds, so this is that lang item's test |
| `EF-04`: on a freestanding target with no `@globalAllocator`, `report(r: Result<u8>)`, which drops `r`, and `return Err("disk timeout");`; `readSector(lba)?` into `Result<u8>` with `DiskError.BadSector { lba }` | Compiles with no EN0820: every `Failure` there has `cap == 0`, so its drop glue frees nothing; EN0820 at the `?`, `` turning `DiskError.BadSector` into a text needs memory, and this kernel has no heap ``, with the label `` `BadSector` has fields, so its text is built at run time `` and `` help: return `Result<u8, DiskError>` to pass the error on as it is `` (twin: E0425, Rust's `no_std` has no `Box<dyn Error>`) |
| `OQ21-sector`: on the same target, `return Err(DiskError.Timeout);` in `Result<u8>`; `?` into `Result<u8>` on a `Result<u8, BusError>` whose cases have no fields; `return Err(DiskError.BadSector { lba });` in `Result<u8>`; a `load` returning `Result<u8, DiskError>` with `return readSector(lba)?;`; ``return Err(`bad sector ${lba}`);`` in `Result<u8>` | Compiles: a case with no fields, or with a text, converts to a static text with `cap == 0`; compiles; EN0820 at the `Err`; compiles, and the error keeps its fields until it is printed; EN0820 at the template, with `` help: use a fixed text, or return an enum error type whose case holds the value `` |
| `S2-32-lift`: on the same target, `private function lift<T, E>(r: Result<T, E>): Result<T> { return r?; }`, called from a `boot(): Result<u8>` as `lift(probe())?` with `probe(): Result<u8, BusError>` whose cases have no fields, as `lift(readSector(7))?` with the `readSector` of `EF-04`, and as `lift(ready())?` with `ready(): Result<u8>` returning `Err("not ready")` | Compiles for `lift<u8, BusError>`; EN0820 for `lift<u8, DiskError>`, an instance-dependent code like EN0414 and EN0435, reported in phase 5 with the reach chain, its primary label at `lift(readSector(7))` and a note at the `?` in `lift`, with a help that names the generic form, `return Result<T, E>`; compiles for `lift<u8, Failure>`, whose `?` moves the error as it is, with no call to `failure_from_display` and an allocation count of 0 (twin: E0277 `` `?` couldn't convert the error: `E: StaticText` is not satisfied `` at the `?` when unbounded, and E0277 `` the trait bound `DiskError: StaticText` is not satisfied `` at the call with a `where Failure: From<E>` bound) |
| `EF-06`: `Formatter.fromFn(bufSink, ctx)` with `private unsafe function bufSink(ctx: *u8, bytes: *u8, n: usize): void`; the same with a safe `bufSink` that reads through `bytes`; a method that reads through a raw-pointer parameter whose signature a safe interface member, or a safe base method, fixes | Compiles with no warning, because the sink type is `unsafe fn(...)`; warning EN0828, and with its `unsafe function` help applied it compiles with none (clippy reports `not_unsafe_ptr_arg_deref`); compiles with no EN0828, since neither half of its help could be followed there |
| `EF-08`: `Result<i64, &AgeError>`; `Result<i64, Lexer>` with a borrowing `Lexer` | EN0701 once each, with no EN0454 |
| `EF-07`: `handler?: (k: u8) => void`, called as `onKey(66, k => console.log(k))`; `width?: i64 \| null` with `const w: i64 = width ?? 8;`; `public class Opts { public width?: i64 \| null; }` with `const o = new Opts {}; console.log(o.width ?? 8);`; that field written `public width?: i64 \| null = 3;` | Compiles each: the first means `handler: ((k: u8) => void) \| null = null`, and the second `width: i64 \| null = null`, with no `null` inside a `null`, so `width ?? 8` is an `i64`; compiles and prints `8`, because a field with `?` collapses the same way; EN0227, since a field has both `?` and a default |
| `EP-06-opt`: `public type Opt<T> = { v?: T };` with `private function put<T>(o: &Opt<T>, x: T): void { o.v = x; }`, then `let a: Opt<i64 \| null> = { v: 5 }; put<i64 \| null>(&a, null);`; `const w: { v?: i64 \| null } = a;`; a class field `public v?: T` at `T = i64 \| null` | Compiles, and `a.v` holds a present `null`: the collapse is decided on the type as written where `?` is written, and a type parameter is never already nullable there, so `Opt<i64 \| null>` is `{ v: (i64 \| null) \| null }`; EN0401, because a written `{ v?: i64 \| null }` is `{ v: i64 \| null }`; the field is `(i64 \| null) \| null` the same way |
| `OQ24-onKey`: `onKey(65); onKey(66, k => console.log(k));` with `private function onKey(key: u8, handler?: (k: u8) => void): void { if (handler != null) { handler(key); } }`; the same body calling `handler(key)` with no test; `pad(); pad(3);` with `private function pad(width?: i64 \| null): void { const w: i64 = width ?? 8; console.log(w); }` | Prints `66` (TypeScript and Swift print `66`); EN0428, because a callback that may be `null` is not callable until narrowed; prints `8` then `3` |

**Reload scenarios from the same review,** each declaring `covers` for its `DEV_RUNTIME.md` row:

| Scenario | Must give |
|---|---|
| `D20E-07`: v1 `main` runs `cur.set(&xs[0]);` and then a sleeping loop that prints `cur.get()`; v2 deletes that line and adds `xs.push(7);` to the loop | Loop-head replacement is refused at the back edge, because `cur` is a live borrowing local holding v1's loans, so the frame keeps its old body (twin of v2's loop after v1's `set`: E0502). Covers ADV-56 and ADV-51 |
| `D20E-08`: v2 makes `rewind(s: &string)` store `s` and calls it with a literal, while a `step` frame parked in `os.sleep` will still call `lx.rewind(&tmp)` | The parked call binds the retired v1 `rewind`, because the contract in `sigHash` includes stores and through_fields; no fault under `--oracle`. Covers ADV-59 and REF-10 |
| `F10`: v2 makes both `speak` bodies only read, while a `Dog` built under v1 sits in a `Box<Animal>[]` and a new `tick` keeps a part of `p.name` across `p.speak()`; the reverse edit, with an old `tick` frame parked between taking its part and calling `p.speak()` | At commit the `Dog`'s method-table pointer moves to v2's table, so `p.speak()` runs v2's `Dog.speak` and nothing faults; the parked frame, which will reach a call through a changed table entry, blocks the reload until it returns. Covers ADV-57, ADV-58, MIG-15 and REF-13 |
| `F2`: v2 gives `Dog` a field while a `Box<Animal>[]` holds a `Dog`, and `Animal` is unchanged; the list is dropped after the reload | The box migrates by the class its table names, so its block grows to `Dog`'s new size and its hidden pointer moves to v2's table; the later free reads the size v2's table records, with no census failure and no `Bad free` under `--oracle`. Covers ADV-60 and MIG-14 |
| `S01`: v2 makes a `Cursor` method push through its reference field, which turns on the class's flag for writing through its reference fields, while `main` holds a live `cur` whose v1 `set` took a shared loan | Blocking frame, even though no field changed: the reload stays pending until the frame returns or the user restarts, and the status line names the frame. Covers ADV-61 |
| `S01-built-after`: the `server.en` of `S01` with `private final class Cursor { private v: &i64[]; private n: isize; public constructor(v: &i64[]) { this.v = v; } public step(): void { this.n += 1; } }` and a `main` that runs `let data: i64[] = [1, 2, 3]; os.sleep(5000); const e = &data[0]; let cur = new Cursor(&data); cur.step(); console.log(*e);`; v2, saved while `main` is parked in `os.sleep`, before `let cur = new Cursor(&data);`, makes `step` run `this.v.push(0);` as well and moves `console.log(*e);` above `let cur`, since v2 rejects the old order | Blocking frame: `main`'s reachable code builds a `Cursor`, whose flag v2 turns on, with the shared loan its old check gave, so the reload stays pending until `main` returns, and the status line names the frame (twin of v1's `main` against v2's `Cursor`: E0502 `` cannot borrow `data` as mutable because it is also borrowed as immutable ``). Covers ADV-75 |
| `S01-pinned`: the same v2 while a live function value names a retired body that builds a `Cursor`; the same v2 while the only parked frame's reachable code never builds a `Cursor` or stores into one | Refused with EN1213, naming the value's path, since waiting cannot help; commits at once; `S01` is unchanged |
| `OQ15-reload`: v2 adds a method to `Animal` while a frame parked in `os.sleep` still has `pet.unbox()` ahead, once with an `Animal` in the `Box<Animal>` and once with a `Dog` | Commit moves the hidden pointer to v2's table, and the rebased frame's exact-class check compares it with v2's `Animal` table: the `Animal` unboxes with no false panic, and the `Dog` still panics `` `unbox` expected an `Animal`, but the box holds a `Dog` `` and pauses. Covers ADV-62 |
| `OQ17-reload`: the `OQ17-introduce` loop, with v2 giving `Dog` a field while a frame of `introduce`'s dynamic copy, holding `s` for the `Dog`, is parked in `os.sleep` before `s.speak()` | The object word of `s` moves to the migrated `Dog` and its table word to v2's `Dog` table for `Speaker`, which the census counts once, and the call prints `woof`; no word names v1's table after commit, and no fault under `--oracle`. Covers ADV-63, MIG-15 and REF-13 |
| `OQ16-dev`: during `enrang dev`, the interface file of a sourceless `lib` is replaced by the forged file of `OQ16-grow` | EN0316, naming the file, with `still running vN`, and the old program keeps running. Covers FAIL-01 |
| `OQ19-reload`: a loop parked in `os.sleep` prints `h` each turn, with the `Header` of `OQ19-header` and no `fmt`; v2 adds `public ver: u8 = 1;` after `flags`; v3 moves `flags` before `len` | After each commit the next line follows the new schema, as drop glue does: `Header { len: 3, flags: 0, ver: 1 }`, then `Header { flags: 0, len: 3, ver: 1 }`, because the printer of each version's `Header` is made from that version's declaration. Covers MIG-01 and MIG-04 |

**The second attack review.** The programs of the second attack review of 2026-11-18 are permanent fixtures too, named after its synthesis IDs `S2-01` to `S2-33` with a word, as in `S2-08-copy`, or, for a minor finding, after its report ID, as in `BC-06` or `SL-08-cmp`. Each outcome follows the rules as fixed after that review and the owner's answers to its sixteen questions, recorded on 2026-11-18, traced by hand, and a row names its twin's real result where one was run, with `rustc` 1.97.1 or the C++, Swift, Java or Node program the review or the answer names. The programs those answers add beyond the rows they settle are permanent fixtures too, named `Q` with the number of the owner's recorded decision and a word, as in `Q31-ctor-order` or `Q47-escapes`, and each sits beside the row it settles. Rows about the `for...of` hold sit in that table above (`S2-14-const`, `S2-23-rerun`, `S2-24-narrowed`, `S2-25-pair` and `LTK-5`, and `LTK-4` in the row on nested writing loops, which keeps EN0707 because another loop's hold reports by mode). Rows that extend an existing row sit beside it: `S2-22-copy` and `S2-22-string` beside `OQ25-order`, `S2-33-err` beside `OQ20-age`, `S2-32-lift` beside `OQ21-sector` and `EP-06-opt` beside `EF-07`. Rows that the fixes change were changed in place: `INF-01`, `INF-02`, `D20E-06` and `OQ12-cost` by `WD2-3`, `INF-08` by `RR-06`, `F2` by `WD2-5`, `MR-2` and `OQ13-slice` by `SL-10`, `F13`, `OQ19-values`, `EP-07`, `S2-02-dtor` and `S2-02-box` by `EP-09`, `OQ19-address` by `MD-7`, `INF-09` by `S2-25-pair`, the `keep` loop run twice by `S2-23-rerun`, the row of `for...of` source forms by `S2-14-const`, and the `slice` row of the named fixtures by `SL-11-bounds`. `S2-01-transitive`, `S2-11-final`, `S2-11-abstract`, `S2-18-unsafe`, `Q34-types` and `Q44-hash` are interface-file meta-tests below, and `S2-19-pinned`, `S2-20-late`, `S2-21-extends`, `S2-21-make` and `SL-12-views` are reload scenarios. `S2-31` was refuted and has no row. A string nested inside another value prints in single quotes (`EP-09`), in these rows and in the tables above. Accepted programs also run under `--oracle`, and any fault is a soundness bug.

Borrowing values, stores and results:

| Fixture | Must give |
|---|---|
| `S2-04-keep`: a `Holder` with `private r: &i64[]`, `public keep(x: &i64[]): &Slice<i64> { this.r = x; return x.slice(0, 1); }` and a `grow()` that pushes 100 times through `this.r`; `let h = new Holder(&a); const k = h.keep(&v); h.grow(); console.log(k);` with `let v: i64[] = [1, 2]` | EN0708 at `x.slice(0, 1)` in `keep`: `Holder` writes through `r`, so the store borrows `x` for writing, and that borrow stays live until `keep` returns, because `h` outlives the call; so no call gives `k` and `h` one loan while `h` may write through it (twin E0502 in `keep` at `&x[0..1]`) |
| `S2-04-aim`: `function aim(c: &Cursor, xs: &i64[]): &Slice<i64> { c.set(xs); return xs.slice(0, 1); }` with a `Cursor` whose `set(xs: &i64[])` stores `xs` and whose `grow()` pushes through it | EN0708 at `xs.slice(0, 1)` in `aim`: `set` borrows `xs` for writing, and the store into `c` keeps that borrow live until `aim` returns |
| `S2-04-reset`: the `Lexer` of SPEC 5.1 with `public reset(s: &string): &string \| null { this.src = s; this.pos = 0; return s.slice(0, 1); }`; `const source = "x"; const text = "abc"; let lx = new Lexer(&source); const first = lx.reset(&text); const t = lx.next(); console.log(first, t);` | Compiles and prints `a abc`: `Lexer` only reads through `src`, so neither `first` nor `lx` writes through the loan on `text` they share, and `lx.next()` meets no loan that `first` holds (twin with `fn reset(&mut self, s: &'a str) -> Option<&'a str>` prints `a abc`) |
| `S2-06-default`: `private function visit(xs: &i64[], step: () => void = () => { xs.push(0); }): void { const head = xs.first(); step(); console.log(head); }`, called as `visit(&v)` with `const v: i64[] = [1, 2, 3, 4]` | EN0608 at `&v`, `` `visit` can change `xs`, but `v` is `const` ``, and EN0715 at `xs.first()`, `` `xs` is locked while `step` will still be used ``: a default belongs to the callee's body, which evaluates it first when a call leaves the parameter out, so `xs` is Write and `step` holds the default arrow's capture (twin, with the default lowered into the callee: E0501 at `xs.first()` and E0596 at `visit(&mut v, None)`) |
| `S2-06-grow`: `private function f(xs: &i64[], n: isize = grow(xs)): void {}` with a `grow` that pushes onto its parameter; `f(&c)` with `const c: i64[] = [1]`; `const e = &w[0]; f(&w); console.log(*e);` with `let w: i64[] = [1]` | EN0608 at `&c`; EN0706 at `&w`: the default's call is part of `f`'s body, so `xs` is Write |
| `S2-07-ref`: `let data: i64[] = [1, 2, 3]; const e = &data[0]; let pair: [&i64[], i32] = [&data, 1]; const t = &pair; t[0].push(4); console.log(*e);` | EN0706 at `&data`: a borrow of a borrowing tuple held directly, into a slot that is written through later, makes every reference part of the tuple written at that point, so the part's loan is exclusive while `e` reads (twin E0502 with a `&mut String` part; E0596 `` cannot borrow `*t.0` as mutable, as it is behind a `&` reference `` with a shared part) |

| `C4-abstract-dtor`: `public abstract class Shape` with `public abstract area(): i64;`, `` public describe(): string { return `area ${this.area()}`; } `` and `` public destructor() { console.log(`bye, ${this.describe()}`); } ``, and `public class Sq extends Shape { public side: i64; public override area(): i64 { return this.side * this.side; } }`; `{ const q = new Sq { side: 2 }; console.log(q.describe()); }` | Prints `area 4`, then panics `` `Shape.area` is abstract, and the subclass that implements it is already cleaned up ``, with exit status 101 on hosted cells, and `enrang dev` pauses on it as on any panic: `Shape`'s destructor runs with `Shape`'s table in the hidden pointer (`S2-02-dtor`), and every abstract slot of an abstract class's own table and of its interface tables points at a stub that panics, never at nothing, so the VM reports no Bad function pointer (g++ twin, a non-virtual helper that reaches a pure virtual function from the base destructor, prints `live: 4`, `pure virtual method called` and `terminate called without an active exception`, exit status 134; the Swift twin, whose deinit keeps the subclass's dispatch, prints `live: 4`, `bye, area 4` and `end`) |
| `C4-drop-order`: `` private class Noisy { public constructor(private tag: string) { console.log(`make ${this.tag}`); } public destructor() { console.log(`drop ${this.tag}`); } } ``, an `Animal` with `private a: Noisy = new Noisy("animal field");`, a constructor that prints `Animal ctor` and a destructor that prints `Animal dtor`, and a `Dog extends Animal` with no constructor, `private d: Noisy = new Noisy("dog field");` and a destructor that prints `Dog dtor`; `{ const x = new Dog(); }` | Prints `make dog field`, `make animal field`, `Animal ctor`, `Dog dtor`, `drop dog field`, `Animal dtor` and `drop animal field`: a subclass's fields are created before its base's (`Q31-ctor-order`), while its destructor and fields still go before its base's, as in C++, so cleanup is not the reverse of creation across levels (SPEC 5.7 D2) (g++ twin prints `make animal field`, `Animal ctor`, `make dog field`, `Dog dtor`, `drop dog field`, `Animal dtor` and `drop animal field`; the Swift twin, whose construction order this is, prints `make dog field`, `make animal field`, `Animal init`, `Dog init`, `Dog deinit`, `Animal deinit`, `drop animal field` and `drop dog field`) |
| `S2-02-ctor`: an `Animal` with `public name: string;`, whose constructor runs `this.name = name;` and then prints `` `hello, ${this.describe()}` ``, whose `describe` returns `` `${this.name} makes a sound` `` and whose destructor prints `` `bye, ${this.describe()}` ``, and a `Dog` whose `describe` override returns `` `${this.name} of ${this.owner.name}` `` from a `private owner: Box<Person>` that its constructor assigns after `super(name)`; the same `Dog` with `this.owner = owner;` before `super(name)`, in `{ const rex = new Dog("Rex", new Box(new Person("Ada"))); }`, with `public class Person { public constructor(public name: string) {} }`; `console.log(this)` after `this.name = name;` in `Animal`'s constructor | EN0610 at `super(name)`, because `owner` has no default and is not yet assigned there, with the help to assign it before `super(name)` (`Q31-before-super`); compiles and prints `hello, Rex of Ada` then `bye, Rex makes a sound`: a subclass's own fields are set before its base part is built, so a call through `this` in a base constructor runs the object's own method, `Dog.describe`, on fields already set, while the base's destructor runs `Animal.describe`, after `Dog`'s fields are gone (twins in `Q31-ctor-order`); prints `Dog { name: 'Rex', owner: Person { name: 'Ada' } }`, through `Dog`'s printer, as everywhere else |
| `Q31-ctor-order`: an `Animal` with `` public constructor(public name: string) { console.log(`ctor: ${this.describe()}`); } ``, `` public describe(): string { return `${this.name} makes a sound`; } `` and a destructor that prints `` `dtor: ${this.describe()}` ``, and a `Dog` with no constructor, `protected tricks: string[] = ["sit", "roll"];` and `` public override describe(): string { return `${this.name} knows ${this.tricks[0]}`; } ``; ``{ const d = new Dog("Rex"); console.log(`live: ${d.describe()}`); }``; a `Puppy extends Dog` with `private months: i64;`, `public constructor(name: string, months: i64) { this.months = months; super(name); }` and a `describe` override that returns `` `${this.name}, ${this.months} months, knows ${this.tricks[0]}` ``, as `new Puppy("Rex", 3)` | Prints `ctor: Rex knows sit`, `live: Rex knows sit` and `dtor: Rex makes a sound`: a subclass with no constructor sets its fields' defaults, then runs its base's constructor, whose call through `this` runs `Dog.describe` on `tricks` already set, while the base's destructor still runs `Animal`'s code (Swift twin, whose order this is, prints `init: Rex knows sit`, `live: Rex knows sit` and `deinit: Rex knows sit`; the C++ twin prints `ctor: Rex makes a sound`, `live: Rex knows sit` and `dtor: Rex makes a sound`; Node, which sets `tricks` after `Animal`'s constructor returns, throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading '0')`); the `Puppy` prints `ctor: Rex, 3 months, knows sit`: a chain is built level by level, most derived first, `Puppy`'s fields, then `Dog`'s, then `Animal`'s |

} super(name); }`, then `{ let d = new Dog("Rex", true); d.tricks.push("sit"); }`; over EP4-01's `Animal`, with `public name: string;`, `public sound: string = "...";` and `public constructor(name: string) { this.name = name; over an `Animal` whose constructor takes `age: i64`, a `Dog` constructor `(age: i64)` that runs `super(age); super(age);`; over one whose constructor takes nothing, a `Dog` constructor `(n: i64)` that runs `let i = 0; } else { super(name); }` and `if (bad) { panic("no"); } super(name);` in constructors `(name: string, quiet: bool)` and `(name: string, bad: bool)` | EN0619 at the `return`, `` `super(name)` isn't called on every path before the constructor returns ``; EN0619 at the closing brace, with the note `` when `quiet` is true ``; EN0610 at `this`, where `super(...)` may not have run, beside that EN0619; EN0619 at the second call, `` `super(age)` may already have run here ``; EN0619 at `super()`, which the back edge reaches after it ran, and at the closing brace, which the loop's zero-turn path reaches without it; EN0619 at the arrow's `super(name)`, since `super(...)` is a statement of the constructor's own body only, never inside an arrow or a local function; EN0219, reported by phase 2 before phase 3 runs, since only a class with a base calls `super(...)`; both compile. Init dataflow tracks `super(...)` as a pseudo-path that every normal exit, a `return` or the closing brace, must have run exactly once, while a path that ends in a panic needs nothing, so no object leaves its constructor with its base part unbuilt or built twice (Swift twin: `'super.init' isn't called on all paths before returning from initializer` for the early return and the loop, and `'super.init' called multiple times in initializer` for the second call and the loop; tsc 6.0.3 `--strict` catches only a missing call, TS2377 `Constructors for derived classes must contain a 'super' call.`; Node v24.18.0 throws `ReferenceError: Must call super constructor in derived class before accessing 'this' or returning from derived constructor` and `ReferenceError: Super constructor may only be called once`) |

this.owner = o; } }`, then `console.log(new Circle(2, new Person("Ada")).describe());`; the same with `super(1)`; the same without `this.owner = o;`; the same with `this.owner = o;` before `super();`;
