| `Q44-show`: `show(&sector); show(sector.slice(0, 2));` with G04's `show<T>(x: &T)` and `let sector: u8[4] = [7; 4];`; `pair(sector.slice(0, 2))` with `private function pair<T>(x: &T): void { console.log(sizeOf<T>(), x); }` | Prints `[7, 7, 7, 7]` then `[7, 7]`: a type parameter that the signature and body use only behind `&` may stand for a view, a fact inferred from the body, as a contract is, and stated with the function's declaration (SPEC 4.10), and in that instance `&T` is two words, the address and the length (twin with `?Sized` prints `[7, 7, 7, 7]` then `[7, 7]`, and without it is E0277 `` the size for values of type `[u8]` cannot be known at compilation time ``; Node prints `Uint8Array(4) [ 7, 7, 7, 7 ]` then `Uint8Array(2) [ 7, 7 ]`); EN0401, `` `T` can't stand for the view `Slice<u8>` ``, with a label at `sizeOf<T>()`, the use that needs a value, and the help to add `pair<T>(x: &Slice<T>)`, which, once added, `pair(sector.slice(0, 2))` runs, since of two generic overloads that both match without a conversion the more specific one wins (`VI-07-overloads`) |
| `VI-02-measured`: `private function show<T extends Measured>(x: &T): void { const m: &Measured = x; console.log(m.length()); }` with `public interface Measured { length(): isize; }`, called as `show(sector.slice(0, 2))` over `const sector: u8[4] = [7, 7, 7, 7];`; `show(s.slice(1, 3))` and `show(&s)` over `let s = "hello".toString();`; `public interface Sizer { size<T extends Measured>(x: &T): isize; }` and a `public class LenSizer implements Sizer` whose `public size<T extends Measured>(x: &T): isize { const m: &Measured = x; return m.length(); }` | EN0401, `` `T` can't stand for the view `Slice<u8>` ``, with a label at `const m: &Measured = x;`: a C22 conversion of `T`, `&T` or `Box<T>` is a use that needs a value type, since a two-word `&I` has no room for a view's length (twin with `T: ?Sized`: E0277 `` the size for values of type `T` cannot be known at compilation time `` with `` = note: required for the cast from `&T` to `&dyn Debug` ``; the sized form prints `[7, 7, 7, 7] 16`); EN0401 at each call, `` a `&string` can't become a `&Measured`, because it may be a part of a string ``, labelled at `const m: &Measured = x;`, with `` help: add `show(x: &string)` ``: the body loses the fact that `T` may stand for `string`, since the instance's `&T` would be a pointer and a length with no whole descriptor for the `&Measured` (twin: with `T: Measured + ?Sized` and `impl Measured for str`, the body is E0277 `` the size for values of type `T` cannot be known at compilation time `` with `` = note: required for the cast from `&T` to `&dyn Measured` ``, as for the view); EN0401 at `const m: &Measured = x;` in `LenSizer`, with `` note: `Sizer.size` lets `T` stand for `string` ``, since a call through the member may bind `string` |
| `VI-02-cast`: `public function after<T>(x: &T): usize { const p = x as *T; return p.add(1) as usize - p as usize; }`, called as `after(sector.slice(0, 2))` | EN0401 the same way, with a label at `x as *T`: a type parameter that may stand for a view stands only behind `&`, so the fact is lost at any `*T`, a cast, a local, a parameter, a field, `ptr.null<T>()` or a pointer method (twin E0277 at `wrapping_add` on a `*const T` with `T: ?Sized`, and E0271 for `std::ptr::null::<T>()`) |
| `VI-02-class`: `public class Keeper<T extends Measured> { public show(x: &T): void { const m: &Measured = x; console.log(m.length()); } }` with the `Measured` of `VI-02-measured`, then `const k = new Keeper<string> {}; k.show(s.slice(1, 3));` over `let s = "hello".toString();` | EN0401 at `Keeper<string>`, `` a `&string` can't become a `&Measured`, because it may be a part of a string ``, labelled at `const m: &Measured = x;`: a class's type parameter may stand for `string` only while no body of the class converts its `&T` to an interface reference, since in the instance the `&T` that `k.show` receives is a pointer and a length, with no whole descriptor for the `&Measured` (twin with `struct Keeper<T: ?Sized>` and `impl<T: Measured + ?Sized> Keeper<T>`: E0277 `` the size for values of type `T` cannot be known at compilation time `` with `` = note: required for the cast from `&T` to `&dyn Measured` ``) |
| `VI-02-arrow`: `private function show<T extends Measured>(x: &T): void { each(() => { const m: &Measured = x; console.log(m.length()); }); }` with `private function each(f: () => void): void { f(); }`, called as `show(s.slice(1, 3))` | EN0401 at the call, the same message, labelled at the conversion inside the arrow: an arrow nested in a body, at any depth, counts as part of it for the facts that a type parameter may stand for a view or for `string`, though the borrow check takes it as its own body (twin with `T: Measured + ?Sized` and the conversion in a closure: E0277 `` the size for values of type `T` cannot be known at compilation time `` with `` = note: required for the cast from `&T` to `&dyn Measured` ``) |
| `VI-02-structural`: `public interface Measured { length(): isize; }`, `public interface Sizer { size<T extends Measured>(x: &T): isize; }` and a `public class Keeper` with no `implements` whose `public size<T extends Measured>(x: &T): isize { const m: &Measured = x; return m.length(); }`, then `let s = "hello".toString(); const k = new Keeper(); f(&k, s.slice(1, 3));` with `function f(z: &Sizer, v: &string): void { console.log(z.size(v)); }`; `Keeper.size` returning `0` without the conversion | EN0401 at `const m: &Measured = x;` in `Keeper.size`, `` a `&string` can't become a `&Measured`, because it may be a part of a string ``, with `` note: `Keeper` is used as a `Sizer` here `` at `f(&k, s.slice(1, 3))` and `` note: `Sizer.size` lets `T` stand for `string` ``: satisfaction is one relation, so its string clause runs at a structural use too, where a check keyed on `implements` let the two-word part become the object word of an `&Measured` (twin with `T: Measured + ?Sized`: E0277 `` the size for values of type `T` cannot be known at compilation time ``); compiles and prints `0`, and so do the forms of `VI-02-inherited` and `VI-02-bound` with that `size` |
| `VI-02-inherited`: the interfaces of `VI-02-structural`, a `public class Base` whose `size` runs `const m: &Measured = x;`, and `public class Keeper extends Base implements Sizer {}`, with the same `f(&k, s.slice(1, 3))` | EN0401 at `Base.size`'s conversion, with `` note: `Keeper` is used as a `Sizer` here `` at `implements Sizer`: the member checked is the one lookup finds, an inherited one included (twin E0277) |
| `VI-02-bound`: the `Keeper` of `VI-02-structural`, with `g(&k, s.slice(1, 3))` and `function g<Z extends Sizer>(z: &Z, v: &string): void { console.log(z.size(v)); }`; the same `Keeper` with `implements Sizer` and `f(&k, s.slice(1, 3))` | EN0401 at the conversion, with `` note: `Keeper` is used as a `Sizer` here `` at `g(&k, s.slice(1, 3))`, where the inferred `Z := Keeper` meets the bound (twin E0277); EN0401 at the conversion, unchanged |
| `FIT-structural`: a `public class Tally` with no `implements`, a `private n: isize;` and `public size<T extends Measured>(x: &T): isize { this.n += 1; return this.n; }`, passed to the `f(z: &Sizer, v: &string)` of `VI-02-structural` as `f(&t, &s)` on a `let t = new Tally();` | EN0615 at `Tally`, `` `size` changes the object, but `Sizer` declares that it doesn't ``: phase 3 fits every recorded satisfaction, a structural one included, not only a class with an `implements` clause |
| `VI-05-dumper`: `public interface Dumper { dump<T>(x: &T): void; }` implemented by `public final class Hex`, whose `dump<T>(x: &T)` prints `sizeOf<T>()` and the object's bytes through `Slice<u8>.fromRaw` in `unsafe`, and `private function dumpWith(d: &Dumper, v: &Slice<u8>): void { d.dump(v); }`, called as `dumpWith(&h, sector.slice(0, 2))` | EN0401 at `d.dump(v)`, `` `T` can't stand for the view `Slice<u8>` ``, with a note that `Dumper.dump` has no body: a declaration with no body, an interface member, an abstract method, a `declare function` or an `@intrinsic` member the compiler's table does not cover, lacks the view fact, so the body that runs, which may use `T` by value, is never reached with a view (twin: an impl that drops the trait's `?Sized` is E0276 `impl has stricter requirements than trait`, one that keeps it but calls `size_of::<T>()` is E0277 `` the size for values of type `T` cannot be known at compilation time ``, and a conforming one prints `[7, 7]` and `[7, 7, 7, 7]`) |
| `VI-05-override`: `public class Animal { public show<T>(x: &T): void { console.log(x); } }` and a `Dog extends Animal` with `public override show<T>(x: &T): void { console.log(sizeOf<T>()); }`; the same override with `` console.log(`dog ${x}`); `` as its body | EN0460 at each override, whatever its body does, `` `show` has its own type parameters, so it can't be overridden ``, with `help: give Dog's method another name`: a method compiled once per type has no method-table slot, so a call through `&Animal` always runs `Animal.show`, never one of `Dog`'s bodies with a view, so no override needs a view check of its own (`VI-06-tables`) |
| `VI-07-overloads`: `describe(&rex)` with `describe(a: &Animal)`, which prints `` `describe(Animal): ${a.name}` ``, and `describe<T extends Animal>(a: &T)`, which prints `` `describe<T>: ${a.name}` ``, `rex` a `Dog` named `Rex`; `total(&arr)` with `total(xs: &Slice<i64>)`, which prints `total(Slice)`, and `total<T>(x: &T)`, which prints `total<T>`, `arr` an `i64[3]`; `show(sector.slice(0, 2))` and `show(&sector)` with `show<T>(x: &T)`, which runs `console.log("any:", x);`, and `show<T>(x: &Slice<T>)`, which runs `console.log("view:", x);`, over `let sector: u8[4] = [7; 4];`; the same with `show<T>(x: &T)` running `console.log("any:", x, sizeOf<T>());`; `f(&a3, &b3)` with `f<T>(a: &T, b: &Slice<i64>)` and `f<T>(a: &Slice<i64>, b: &T)`, `a3` and `b3` two `i64[3]` | Prints `describe(Animal): Rex`: among the overloads a call matches, SPEC 4.14 prefers, in order, a non-generic overload to a generic one, then, within the same kind, one that needs no chain to one that needs a chain, then, among generic overloads still tied, the more specific one, where F is more specific than G when every parameter type of F can be passed to G's, G's type parameters bound by F's types, but not the reverse, and the order never reads the view fact; prints `total(Slice)`, the non-generic overload, although it needs the C18 step and `total<T>` none; prints `view: [7, 7]`, since both `show`s are generic and need no chain, and `&Slice<T>` can be passed to `&T` but not the reverse; prints `any: [7, 7, 7, 7]`, since only `show<T>(x: &T)` needs no chain; prints `view: [7, 7]` and `any: [7, 7, 7, 7] 4`, with no EN0401, because the ranking sends the view to the `&Slice<T>` overload without reading the view fact; EN0451, `no overload, or more than one, matches the call`, listing both `f`s, since each is generic and needs one C18 step, and neither is more specific than the other (swiftc 6.4 prints `describe(Animal): Rex`, and `any: [7, 7, 7, 7]` then `view: [7, 7]` for `show` over an array and a slice of it; javac 21 refuses to declare both `describe`s with `<T extends Animal>`, `name clash: describe(Animal) and <T>describe(T) have the same erasure`, and with `<T> describe(T)` prints `describe(Animal): Rex`; g++ 13.3, which ranks an exact template above a conversion, prints `describe<T>: Rex` and `total<T>`, and `show<T>(&Slice<T>)` for the view; tsc picks the first overload declared, and Node prints `impl got a Dog impl`) |
| `SL-11-bounds`: `console.log(xs.slice(3, 1), xs.slice(-2, 4));` over `let xs: i64[] = [1, 2, 3, 4];`, and the same over `const s = "abcd";` | Prints `[] [3, 4]`, and the parts of `s` are empty and `cd`: a start at or after the end gives an empty view, and a negative index counts from the end, as Node prints `Uint8Array(0) [] Uint8Array(2) [ 3, 4 ] [ 3, 4 ] [ 2, 3, 4 ]` for `subarray(3, 1)`, `subarray(-2, 4)`, `slice(-2, 4)` and `slice(1, 10)` (Rust panics `slice index starts at 3 but ends at 1`) |
| `Q45-node`: over `let v: i64[] = [10, 20, 30, 40, 50];`, `console.log(v.slice(3, 1));`, `console.log(v.slice(-2));`, `console.log(v.slice(1, -1));` and `console.log(v.slice(2, 99));`; `console.log("hello".slice(-3));`; `console.log(v.slice(isize.MIN, isize.MAX));` | Prints `[]`, `[40, 50]`, `[20, 30, 40]` and `[30, 40, 50]`, and `llo`, as Node prints `[]`, `[ 40, 50 ]`, `[ 20, 30, 40 ]`, `[ 30, 40, 50 ]` and `llo`: `slice` on arrays, views and strings follows ECMAScript's index rule, with an end that defaults to `length()`, a negative index counted from the end and at least 0, an index past the end clamped to `length()`, and an empty view when the start is at or after the end, so no `slice` panics, of an array, a view or a string, where a clamped index inside a character moves back to its start (`FV-06-char`, `FV-06-slice`) (Rust panics `slice index starts at 3 but ends at 1` and `range end index 99 out of range for slice of length 5`, and a negative index is E0277); prints `[10, 20, 30, 40, 50]`, the arithmetic done with no overflow. `GenSlice` covers the rest |
| `FV-06-char`: `console.log("città".slice(5, 2));`; `console.log("città".slice(-1));`, where `à` takes bytes 4 and 5 | Prints an empty line, with no panic: the clamped start, 5, falls inside `à` and moves back to 4, the start of that character, and a start at or after the end gives an empty view; prints `à`: `-1` counts from the end to 5, inside `à`, which moves back to 4 the same way, so a string `slice` never panics (Node prints `""` and `"à"`, and the byte length is 6; Rust's `is_char_boundary(5)` is false, `get(5..5)` is `None`, and `&s[5..5]` panics `start byte index 5 is not a char boundary; it is inside 'à' (bytes 4..6 of string)`, while `&s[s.floor_char_boundary(5)..]` is `"à"`) |
| `FV-06-slice`: `console.log("città".slice(0, 5));`; `console.log("città".splitAt(5));` | Prints `citt`: the end, 5, falls inside `à` and moves back to 4, so the part ends on a character boundary, where Node prints `città`, since JavaScript counts UTF-16 units, not bytes, a listed difference from JavaScript, as byte indexes already are for text that is not ASCII; prints `['citt', 'à']`, since `splitAt` takes the same index rule, with no panic (Rust's `floor_char_boundary(5)` is `4`, `&s[..4]` is `"citt"` and `split_at(4)` is `("citt", "à")`; Node's `[s.slice(0, 5), s.slice(5)]` prints `[ 'città', '' ]`) |
| `FV-06-codepoint`: `console.log("città".codePointAt(4));`, then `console.log("città".codePointAt(5));` | Prints `224`, then panics `byte index 5 is not a char boundary` and exits with status 101: `codePointAt` takes a byte index that must be a char boundary (SPEC 8.2), and it is the only operation that still raises this panic (SPEC 9.1), since `slice` and `splitAt` move such an index back to its character's start (Rust's `s[4..].chars().next()` is U+00E0, 224, and `s[5..]` panics `start byte index 5 is not a char boundary; it is inside 'à' (bytes 4..6 of string)` with exit status 101) |

Reload scenarios from the second review, each declaring `covers` for its `DEV_RUNTIME.md` row:

| Scenario | Must give |
|---|---|
| `S2-19-pinned`: `const op: fn(let l: &Labeler, t: &string) => void = measure;` called every turn, with `measure` printing `r` after `const r = l.label(title, &fb); fb = "z";` and `Labeler.label` returning `name`; v2 makes `measure` write `l.count`, so `op` pins the retired `measure`; v3 makes `label` return `fallback` and drops `fb = "z";` from the new `measure` | v1 prints `Dune` each turn (the twin, which prints `r` and `fb`, prints `Dune z`); v2 commits with REF-10's warning; v3 is refused with EN1213, naming `op`, with a restart offered, because the pinned body counts as reachable code at every reload and calls through a table entry whose `sigHash` changed, and the old program keeps running (twin of v3's `label` with the old `measure`: E0506 `` cannot assign to `fb` because it is borrowed ``). Covers ADV-64, FAIL-14 and REF-10 |
| `S2-20-late`: a `tick` frame parked in `os.sleep` has `const f: fn(x: &string) => void = show;` ahead, then `const first = s.slice(0, 1); f(s); console.log(first);`, over `let msg: string = "ab"`; v2 makes `show` run `*x += "!";` before printing and changes `tick` to `const f: fn(let x: &string) => void = show; f(s);` | After commit the old frame's `f` names the retired v1 `show`, the body its own code was compiled against, so it prints `ab` then `a`, with no fault under `--oracle`; the next `tick` runs v2 and prints `ab!`. Covers ADV-65 and REF-10 |
| `S2-21-extends`: v2 removes `extends Animal` from `Dog` while `pets: Box<Animal>[]` holds a v1 `Dog` and `main`, parked in `os.sleep`, will print `p.name` and `p.speak()` for each box; the same with `speak` removed from a `Robot` in a `Box<Speaker>[]`, and with `final` added to `Animal`; then with only `implements Speaker` removed from that `Robot`, which keeps `speak` | Refused with EN1210, naming the path to the box, each of the first three: the heap stays byte-identical and the old program keeps running. The last commits, since `Robot` still satisfies `Speaker` structurally, and the box's table word moves to the v2 `Robot` table for `Speaker`, through which the next turn's `speak` runs. Covers MIG-16, ADV-66, ADV-67 and ADV-68 |
| `S2-21-make`: v2 removes `extends Animal` from `Dog` while no `Dog` is live, and `main`, parked in `os.sleep`, still has `const b: Box<Animal> = new Box(new Dog("Rex")); console.log(b.name);` ahead | The frame blocks, because its reachable code upcasts a `Dog` to an `Animal` it no longer extends, and the reload waits; after 5 s with no walkable point, FAIL-03 names the frame and offers a restart, and the old program keeps running. Covers ADV-69 |
| `SL-12-views`: a frame parked in `os.sleep` holds `const all = ps.slice(0, 1);`, `const tail = ps.slice(ps.length(), ps.length());` and `const none = empty.slice(0, 0);` over `let ps: Point[] = [new Point { x: 1 }]` and `const empty: Point[] = []`, then prints `all` and `tail`; v2 adds `public y: i64 = 0;` to `Point` | Commit retargets `all` to the migrated element, since its one element survives, `tail` by its index to the end of the migrated array, and leaves `none` a dangling pointer aligned for v2's `Point`; the frame prints `[Point { x: 1, y: 0 }] []`, with no fault under `--oracle`. Covers REF-14 |

**The third attack review.** The programs of the third attack review of 2026-11-19 are permanent fixtures too. A row takes the ID under which that review's critics verified its finding, with a word, as in `FV-01-ctor`, `TK-01-dup` or `V-ME-06-err`; where the critics named a row after one of the reports they merged, it takes that report's ID, as in `MD3-01-private` or `S2-30-view`; and the construction rows are `C4-` and a word: `C4-super-paths` (C4-01, with EP4-01), `C4-abstract-dtor` (C4-02), `C4-pprop-default` (C4-03), `C4-ctorless-base` (C4-05), `C4-override-nodefault` (MD3-05, with C4-07), `C4-drop-order` (WD4-03) and `C4-move-inherited` (FR-04). Each outcome follows the rules as fixed after that review, traced by hand, and a row names its twin's real result where one was run, with `rustc` 1.97.1, Swift 6.4, g++ 13.3, tsc 6.0.3, Node v24.18.0 or OpenJDK 21. Thirteen of its findings were left to the owner, C4-05, FR-04, FV-02, FV-05, FV-06, VI-01, VI-06, VI-07, V-ME-05, V-ME-10, V-ME-11, SLB-01 and TK-03, and all were settled on 2026-11-19, FV-06, V-ME-10 and V-ME-11 by the recommendation the owner deferred to: their rows give the decided outcomes. Rows that extend an existing row sit beside it: `TK-01-dup` to `TK-01-local` and `TK-03-first-two` to `TK-03-copied-ref` beside `S2-25-pair`, and `TK-02-reassign-break` beside the loops that repoint their source, in the `for...of` table; `SLB-02-read`, `SLB-02-element`, `SLB-07-narrow`, `SLB-01-value` and `SLB-01-readline` beside `S2-22-string`, `V-ME-09-arrow` beside `EF-10` and `V-ME-06-err` beside `S2-33-err`; `S2-30-view` beside `S2-30-cell`, and `FV-01-ctor`, `FV-01-arrow`, `FV-02-copier` and `FV-05-pad` beside `Q38-readonly-field`; `C4-abstract-dtor` and `C4-drop-order` beside `S2-02-box`, `C4-super-paths` and `C4-ctorless-base` beside `Q31-before-super`, `C4-override-nodefault` beside `Q32-field-override`, `C4-pprop-default` beside `S2-02-init`, `C4-move-inherited` beside `S2-03-into`, `VI-04-copy` beside `S2-03-copy`, `VI-03-swap` and `VI-03-branches` beside `S2-09-self`, `VI-01-part`, `VI-06-tables`, `VI-08-zst` and `VI-09-null` beside `S2-17-pick`, and `V-ME-07-fmt`, `V-ME-08-erased`, `V-ME-10-diff`, `V-ME-10-err` and `V-ME-11-result` beside `Q47-escapes`; and `FV-04-length` and `FV-04-rodata` beside `Q39-from-raw`, `FV-07-const-view` beside `Q41-written`, `VI-02-measured`, `VI-02-cast`, `VI-05-dumper`, `VI-05-override` and `VI-07-overloads` beside `Q44-show`, and `FV-06-char` and `FV-06-slice` beside `Q45-node`. Rows that the fixes change were changed in place: `S2-22-string` by SLB-02, the row of a `keep` loop run once by TK-04, `INF-09`, `S2-25-pair` and the row that repoints a loop's source `r` by TK-02, `S2-26-test` by FV-01, `Q39-from-raw` by FV-04, `Q45-node`, the `slice` row of the named fixtures and `GenSlice` by FV-06, `Q38-readonly-field` by FV-05, `Q44-show` by VI-07, `S2-03-into` by FR-04, `VI-05-override` by VI-06, and section 13's translation and its named disagreements by FV-02 and TK-03. `MD3-01-private`, `MD3-01-override`, `MD3-01-abstract`, `MD3-01-position`, `MD3-01-named`, `MD3-02-guards`, `MD3-03-default`, `MD3-06-modes`, `MD3-06-cpu` and `MD3-06-same-bytes` are interface-file meta-tests below, and the reload scenarios follow. C4-04 and FV-08 were refuted and have no row, and V-ME-12 only brings `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 12 in line with the M3 gate of section 23, which already names G24. The gate on the applied decisions, of the same day, added rows that follow the rules as fixed after it: `TK-03-stale-join` beside `TK-03-copied-ref`, `FV-02-nested`, `FV-02-move` and `FV-02-main` beside `FV-02-copier`, `VI-02-class` and `VI-02-arrow` beside `VI-02-cast`, and `FV-06-codepoint` beside `FV-06-slice`. The soundness pass on the read set, the 'via bind' marks and `readonly` tuple parts, of 2026-11-20, added rows that follow the rules as fixed after it: `TK-01-pick`, `TK-01-owned-fields` and `TK-01-local-ref` beside `TK-01-local`, `TK-03-tail-mixed-join` and `TK-03-two-loops` beside `TK-03-tail-mixed`, and `TK-03-fields-of-turn` beside `TK-03-stale-join`; `INF-04-read-bit`, `INF-04-read-bit-put`, `INF-04-fields-only`, `INF-04-fields-only-take`, `INF-04-set`, `INF-04-take`, `INF-04-fewer` and `INF-04-upcast` beside `INF-04`; `S01-built-after` and `S01-pinned` beside `S01`; `D9-store` beside `FV-01-arrow`; `FV-02-finder`, `FV-02-finder-const`, `RO-loop-const`, `RO-loop-field`, `FV-02-swap`, `FV-02-pick`, `FV-02-factory` and `FV-02-make` beside `FV-02-main`; `FV-05-pad-readset`, `FV-05-pad-flag`, `FV-05-nested` and `FV-05-nested-mark` beside `FV-05-pad`; `C21-inherited` and `C22-loan` beside `S2-16-upcast`; `C18-loan` and `C18-fixed` beside `SL-06-chain`; `VI-02-structural`, `VI-02-inherited`, `VI-02-bound` and `FIT-structural` beside `VI-02-arrow`; and, among the named fixtures, a `.mir` row for `Convert`. `SLB-01-value`, the alias twin of `FV-02-copier` and section 13's translation and named disagreements changed in place. The fixes of that pass's open issues, of 2026-11-21, added `FV-02-alias`, `FV-02-alias-param`, `FV-02-alias-other`, `FV-02-alias-split` and `FV-02-alias-nested` beside `FV-02-make`, `BC-08-readonly` beside `BC-08` and `TK-01-own-view` beside `TK-01-owned-fields`, and changed `TK-01-owned-fields`, `TK-01-pick`, `INF-04-upcast`, `FV-05-pad-flag` and section 13's named disagreements in place. The review of the M3 borrow checker, of 2026-11-22, added `FV-02-part-copy` beside `FV-02-field-copy`, and `HOLD-through` beside the loop that repoints its source `r` in the `for...of` table, and changed `FV-02-skip` and `INF-04-take` in place. Accepted programs also run under `--oracle`, and any fault is a soundness bug.

Reload scenarios from the third review, each declaring `covers` for its `DEV_RUNTIME.md` row:

| Scenario | Must give |
|---|---|
| `C4-06-adopt`: v1 has an `Animal` whose `public constructor(public name: string)` prints `` `hello, ${this.describe()}` ``, a `Dog` with `public constructor(name: string) { super(name); }` and a `describe` override, and an `adopt(n: &string)` frame parked in `os.sleep(5000)` with `const d = new Dog(n.toString()); console.log(d.describe());` ahead; v2 gives `Dog` the field `private owner: Box<Person>;`, the constructor `public constructor(name: string, owner: Box<Person>) { this.owner = owner; super(name); }` and a `describe` that reads `this.owner.name`; the same with `private tricks: string[] = [];` as v2's new field, the constructor still gaining a parameter | Blocking frame: the parked call would bind the retired one-parameter constructor, which never writes `owner`, a field with no default, so the reload waits until `adopt` returns, and nothing reads an uninitialised `Box` under `--oracle`; with `tricks`, the reload commits, and the call binds the retired constructor, whose level first stores `[]` through the field's initialiser shim. Covers ADV-70 |
| `C4-06-pack`: v1's constructor of `Pack`, a class v2 does not make dirty, parked at the back edge of `while (i < n) { i++; }`, after which it runs `this.items = new Box(new Item(i));`; v2, with the same signature, runs `this.items = new Box(new Item(0));` before the loop and again inside it | Loop-head replacement is refused, since a frame running a constructor never takes it, so v2's loop body never drops an `items` that the v1 frame did not set; the frame finishes its v1 body, and the status line names the constructor frame. Covers ADV-71 |
| `C4-override-reload`: an `Animal` with `protected sound: string = "...";` and a constructor that prints `` `ctor: ${this.sound}` ``, as in `Q32-field-override`, and a `Dog` with `public constructor() { os.sleep(5000); super(); }`; v2 adds `protected override sound: string = "woof";` to `Dog` while a v1 `Dog` constructor frame is parked before `super()` | The frame does not block, and its `super()` binds the instance of `Animal`'s constructor for the override set its body named, the empty one, so it prints `ctor: ...` with no fault; a `Dog` built after the commit prints `ctor: woof`. Covers ADV-72 |
| `V-ME-04-tick`: v1 `public class Counter { public n: i64 = 0; public bump(): void { this.n += 1; } }` and `private function tick(c: &Counter): void { os.sleep(100); c.bump(); }`, parked in `os.sleep`, when v2 makes `Counter` `final`; the same edit while `spin(&k)` runs `while (true) { c.bump(); os.sleep(100); }` | Blocking frame: commit would drop the object's hidden pointer, from which `tick`'s old Virtual call would load its table, so the reload waits until `tick` returns and then commits, and nothing faults (the JVM refuses such a reload outright: `java.lang.UnsupportedOperationException: class redefinition failed: attempted to change the class modifiers`, after `v1 n=1`); the loop frame does not block, since after the sleep it reaches the back edge and takes loop-head replacement. Covers ADV-73 and MIG-15 |
| `V-ME-04-make`: v1 `public final class Counter`, with the members of `V-ME-04-tick`, `private function make(): void { os.sleep(100); let c = new Counter(); feed(&c); }` and `private function feed(c: &Counter): void { c.bump(); }`, parked in `os.sleep`, when v2 removes `final` and adds `public class Double extends Counter { public override bump(): void { this.n += 2; } }`; the same `make` with the named form `let c = new Counter { n: 1 };` | Blocking frame: the old `new`, built under `final`, writes no hidden pointer, while `feed`'s latest body, whose `sigHash` is unchanged, calls `c.bump()` through one, so the reload waits until `make` returns, and nothing reads an uninitialised hidden pointer under `--oracle`; the named form does not block, since it writes no table itself and the class's fill function, which it binds by `sigHash`, writes the table under v2's declaration (`ARCHITECTURE.md` section 5). Covers ADV-74 and MIG-15 |
| `VI-09-reload`: `let t: &Speaker \| null = &d; t = null; const u = t;` in a frame parked in `os.sleep`, with the `Dog` of `VI-09-null`, while v2 adds a method to `Dog`, which makes it dirty with its table for `Speaker` (MIG-15) | Commits with no fault under `--oracle`: `null` wrote 0 to both words of `t`, so the typed read of `u`, the census and the retargeting of table words ignore a table word whose object word is 0, and `u`'s is never retargeted. Covers REF-13 and MIG-15 |

**Hermeticity:**
- `Cargo.lock` lists only workspace crates.
- `enrang build` succeeds with an empty `PATH`, so it cannot invoke `cc`, `as` or `ld`.
- Output has no `PT_INTERP` or `DT_NEEDED`.
- PE imports only `kernel32.dll`.
- Two builds are byte-identical.

**Production:** no VM or test machinery in release output; test files left out byte-identically; `@was` and `@devMigrate` inert. **Cross-language:** no compiler-inserted helper reaches itself; no `HostCall` outside `os/vm`; the whole library checks clean per target.

**Interface files:** a cold build equals a warm one, in output and in bytes, including after an imported class is made `final` or has its fields reordered (`F6`), because the hashed API section holds every public declaration that another module's MIR or layouts depend on, so its importers rebuild, and including when that class reaches a module only through another import, as a result type or a re-export, because a module's API hash also covers the API hashes of its direct imports (`S2-01-transitive`: `base` reorders the fields of `Thing`, which the unchanged `lib` returns from `make(): Thing` and the unchanged `mid` prints with `const t = make(); console.log(t.label);`; the warm build rebuilds `mid`, equals the cold one and prints `ada`, and a `mid` with no source is EN0315, as the twin's E0460 `` found possibly newer version of crate `lib` which `mid` depends on `` is); a module's interface API section is identical across checkout paths and with its imports shuffled; a permission flipped in an interface file leaves the output of a build from source unchanged, and makes that file stale, because its own content hash is part of its freshness key, so a warm build rebuilds it from source and equals the cold one (`MR-7`: `grow(&v)` with `first` live is EN0706 both ways after `xs` is flipped to Read in `lib`'s interface file); a module used without its source whose interface file's key no longer matches is EN0315, naming the file, as Rust's E0460 is, never a silent rebuild, unless the key is stale only because contracts or flags changed somewhere in its import closure, so that only direct imports' API hashes moved, while every item the stored code uses, from any module, keeps every other fact it declares, when the module is rechecked against the new contracts instead: its contracts are recomputed from the stored MIR and its borrow check rerun (`OQ16-recheck`: with no source for `mid`, whose `twice(xs: &i64[])` calls `lib`'s `grow(xs)` twice, `lib`'s `grow` changes from reading `xs` to running `xs.push(0)`; `mid` is rechecked, its `twice` now pushes too, and a fresh file is written, so `main`'s `let v: i64[] = [1]; twice(&v); console.log(v);` compiles and prints `[1, 0, 0]`, and keeping `const first = &v[0];` across `twice(&v)` in `main` is EN0706; a `mid` whose stored `twice` keeps `const e = &xs[0];` across `grow(xs)` and then prints `e` fails the recheck with EN0706 at `grow(xs)`, the span its interface file stores, with a note that names that file and says `` this module has no source; it was rechecked against the new `lib` ``, and no file is written; a `lib` whose `grow` also gains a parameter changes the type of an item `mid`'s stored code uses, and a `lib` that no longer has `grow` changes its existence, and `mid` is EN0315 both ways, as it is when the compiler, the target or the file's own contents changed); a module so rechecked, or loaded fresh, runs all of phase 3 over its stored declarations and code after its contracts and flags are recomputed, not only the borrow check, and these cover H35 (`OQ16-refit-override`: `lib` declares `public final class Lexer { private src: &string; public constructor(src: &string) { this.src = src; } public peek(): &string { return this.src.slice(0, 1); } }` and a `Keeper` with `private s: &string;`, `private kept: &Lexer | null = null;`, a constructor storing `s`, `public take(o: &Lexer): void { this.kept = o; }` and `public show(): void { console.log(this.s); }`; a sourceless `mid`, built against it, declares `public class Sub extends Keeper` with `private lx: &Lexer;`, `public override take(o: &Lexer): void { this.lx = o; }` and `public override show(): void { console.log(this.lx.peek()); }`; `lib` v2 changes only the body of `Keeper.take`, to `this.s = o.peek();`, which makes its declared store `(1, 0)` `fields_only`, and `main` runs `attach(&s, &t); s.show();` with `function attach(r: &Keeper, text: &string): void { const tmp = new Lexer(text); r.take(&tmp); }`: the recheck of `mid` is EN0615 at `Sub.take`, `` `Sub.take` keeps `o` itself, but `Keeper.take` keeps only what `o`'s fields hold ``, with the note that names `mid`'s interface file, and no file is written, where rerunning only the borrow check wrote a fresh file and let the `Virtual` call in `attach` apply the narrower store, so `s.lx` pointed into `attach`'s dead frame; the twin `lib` v2 with `fn take(&mut self, o: &Lexer<'a>)` under `mid` v1 is E0460 `` found possibly newer version of crate `lib` which `mid` depends on ``, and `mid` rebuilt against it gives `lifetime may not live long enough` at `self.lx = o`; `OQ16-refit-copy`: `lib`'s `public function touch(xs: &i64[]): void` changes from `console.log(xs.length());` to `xs.push(0);` under a sourceless `mid` whose `public class Cursor implements Copy { private v: &i64[]; public constructor(v: &i64[]) { this.v = v; } public poke(): void { touch(this.v); } public first(): readonly &i64 { return &this.v[0]; } }` was accepted while its flag was off: the recheck recomputes the flag, now on, and is EN0418 at `Cursor`, `` `Cursor` writes through its reference fields, so copying it would make two writers ``, where the rerun of phase 2 saw the stated flag and `main`'s `let c1 = new Cursor(&data); const c2 = c1; const f = c2.first(); c1.poke(); console.log(*f);` read the buffer that `poke` reallocated; twin `#[derive(Clone, Copy)] struct Cursor<'a> { v: &'a mut Vec<i64> }`: E0204 `` the trait `Copy` cannot be implemented for this type ``; `OQ16-refit-narrow`: `lib`'s `public function look(b: &Box2): void`, over `public class Box2 { public v: string | null = null; }`, changes from `console.log(b.v);` to `b.v = null;` under a sourceless `mid` with `public function f(b: &Box2): isize { if (b.v != null) { look(b); return b.v.length(); } return 0; }`: the recheck is EN0401 at `b.v.length()`, naming `look(b)` as the writing line, since the narrowing check reads `look`'s new contract; twin with `if let Some(v) = &b.v { look(b); return v.len(); }` and `fn look(b: &mut Box2)`: E0502 `` cannot borrow `*b` as mutable because it is also borrowed as immutable ``; `OQ16-refit-forged`: with `lib` v2 of `OQ16-refit-override` from the start, a forged fresh `mid` interface file, its key made to match, that states `Sub.take`'s own store `(1, 0)` truthfully is EN0316, naming the file, since the check on load runs fitting over the stored override against `Keeper.take`'s current declared store, where comparing only the stated contracts let it load); a witness stored in an interface file ends at its module's boundary (`OQ16-witness`: `lib`'s `public function grow(xs: &i64[]): void { helper(xs); }` over `private function helper(xs: &i64[]): void { xs.push(0); }`, a `mid` with `public function f(xs: &i64[]): void { grow(xs); }` and a `main` with `const k: i64[] = [1]; f(&k);`, built cold; then `grow` becomes `{ helper(xs); xs.push(0); }`, which keeps every contract and declaration, and a warm build is compared with a cold one of the same sources: both print EN0608 at `&k` with the same witness, `` `f` passes `xs` to `grow` from `lib`, which changes it ``, followed by `lib`'s own `` `grow` pushes to `xs` ``, and both write byte-identical files for `mid`, where `mid`'s file kept the chain through `helper` and the warm build printed it; `rustc` never reuses metadata from an older `lib`, E0460); a used item that changes in any other way than its contract is EN0315 too, never rechecked, because the recheck reruns only phase 3 (`S2-11-final`: `lib` v2 makes `Pet` `final`, which keeps its layout, while a sourceless `mid` declares `public class Puppy extends Pet` and drops a `Box<Pet>` holding a `Puppy`; `S2-11-abstract`: `lib` v2 makes `Shape.area` `abstract` while a sourceless `mid`'s `Blob extends Shape` has no `area`; `mid` is EN0315 both ways, as the twin's E0460 is, and built from source it is EN0442 and EN0446, as the rebuilt twin is E0046 `` not all trait items implemented, missing: `area` ``); a module used from its interface file has its contracts recomputed from the stored code and its borrow check rerun on load, so a fresh file whose stored code does not match its contracts is EN0316, naming the file (`OQ16-grow`: with no source for `lib`, and the key of `lib`'s interface file made to match, the file says `grow` only reads `xs` while its stored code pushes to it, and `main` keeps `const first = &v[0];` across `grow(&v)`; this is EN0316, `` the interface file of `lib.en` says `grow` only reads `xs`, but its code pushes to it ``, with the note `the file was not written by this compiler, or it was changed afterwards`, never a run that reads freed memory); the stored code records its `unsafe` regions explicitly, and the check on load also runs the MIR verifier, in release builds as in tests: the stored code's types against the current declarations, the `unsafe` boundary (EN0801, EN0907, EN0803) against the regions the stored code records, and every phase-2 rule whose verdict phase 3, mono, codegen or the VM rely on, never a fixed list: visibility (EN0308, EN0311) for every field, method, constructor and item the stored code names, the class rules (EN0416, EN0418, EN0442, EN0443, EN0444, EN0445, EN0446, EN0457, EN0458, EN0460), bound satisfaction (EN0419) for every type argument of the stored code and the view and string checks (EN0401) for every one bound to a view or to `string`, and the string clause of satisfaction for every satisfaction the stored code relies on, the construction rules (EN0433, EN0447, EN0448) for every stored `new` and named form, and the position rules (EN0701 to EN0703, EN1006) for every type in the stored declarations and in every local, field, element and generic argument of the stored code; and the verifier requires every run-time guard that `mirbuild` emits to be present, not only where each sits, so stored code that breaks any of them is EN0316, naming the file (`S2-18-unsafe`: a forged interface file of `lib`, its key made to match, whose stored `poke(x: &i64)` writes through `x as *i64` with its `unsafe` region removed and whose stated contract says `poke` only reads `x`, while `main` calls `poke(&n)` on `const n: i64 = 1` with `const r = &n;` live; this is EN0316, `` the interface file of `lib.en` writes through a raw pointer outside `unsafe` in `poke` ``, because the raw write lies outside any recorded `unsafe` region, never a write through a read-only reference; no twin, since `rustc` trusts `.rmeta`; `Q34-types`: a forged interface file of `lib`, its key made to match, whose stored `add(a: i32): i32` returns a `string`, is EN0316 the same way, as OpenJDK 21 rejects a class file whose `add(int)` was edited from `ireturn` to `areturn` with `java.lang.VerifyError: Bad type on operand stack`, and Node's WebAssembly rejects `i32.const` edited to `i64.const` with `CompileError: WebAssembly.Module(): Compiling function #0 failed: type error in fallthru[0] (expected i32, got i64) @+33`; `MD3-01-private`: with `lib`'s `public class View { private r: &i64; ... }`, a forged interface file of a sourceless `mid`, its key made to match, whose stored `poke(v: &View)` writes `99` through `v.r`, which as source is EN0308, while `main` runs `poke(&v)` on `let v = new View(&c)` over `const c: i64 = 5`, is EN0316, never a run that prints `99` for a `const` (OpenJDK 21 links the same shape with `java.lang.IllegalAccessError: class Poke tried to access private field View.r (Poke and View are in unnamed module of loader 'app')`, where the file compiled against a public field printed `wrote 99`); `MD3-01-override`: a stored `public class Dog extends Animal` whose `describe(): i64` overrides `lib`'s `describe(): string`, called through `lib`'s `show(a: &Animal)`, is EN0316; `MD3-01-abstract`: a stored `new Shape()` for `lib`'s `public abstract class Shape`, EN0445 as source, is EN0316; `MD3-01-position`: a stored local `let v: (&i64)[] = [];`, EN0701 as source, that keeps `&x` past `x`'s block, is EN0316; `MD3-01-named`: a genuine named form in another module that leaves out a `private` field with a default loads cleanly, since the declaring module fills that field (`MD3-03-default`), so no private field appears in the importer's code; `MD3-02-guards`: forged stored bodies, one each, that read `(*_1)[_2]` with no `CheckBounds` before the `Index`, project `(*_1 as Present).0` with no `CheckTag`, assign a whole object through `&Animal` with no `CheckClass`, run `Intrinsic SliceFromRaw` outside every recorded `unsafe` region, make a `Callee::Virtual` call through a `final` class with no base, or run `SetTable` on that class, are EN0316 each, never a read 800 KB past a buffer as `at(&v, 100000)` would make, while a guarded `Index` and a `SizeOf` outside `unsafe`, whose kind is safe in the `define_intrinsics!` table, load (Node's WebAssembly, whose bounds check is part of the access, prints `at(8): 0` then `RuntimeError: memory access out of bounds`)); the safety bit of each `define_intrinsics!` kind, which that verifier rule reads, is set exactly when a safe declaration names the kind or `mirbuild` emits it in safe code, checked against every `@intrinsic` declaration and its `unsafe` mark (`ARCHITECTURE.md` section 2.5); the default of a field that a named form leaves out belongs to the declaring module, which fills it through a function of its own, once per set of left-out fields, so no default value is copied into an importer's code (`MD3-03-default`: `lib`'s `Config` with `public retries: i64 = 3;`, a `mid` whose `make()` returns `new Config { verbose: true }`, and `main` printing `make().retries`; `retries` edited to 5, the warm build equals the cold one and prints `5`, a `mid` with no source prints `5` with no EN0315, and a named form in another module that leaves out a `private` field with a default compiles and loads, where the C++ twin with a designated initialiser prints `v1: 3`, `warm (mid.o kept): 3` and `cold (all rebuilt): 5`); a module's own interface file is the same in `ParseMode::Build` and `ParseMode::Test`, since a `.test.en` part is checked from its own source and never stored, so the mode is not part of its freshness key (`MD3-06-modes`: a sourceless `crc.en` whose file `enrang build` wrote is used by `enrang check` and `enrang test` with no EN0315, as one `rlib` with its source deleted serves `rustc` in all three modes, printing `[1, 2, 1, 2]`, `ok` and `test result: ok. 1 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.00s`); `cpu` and `float` are not part of any interface file's freshness key either, with source or without, because nothing the file stores depends on them: its MIR, contracts, flags, view facts, layouts and API hash are the same at every level and float strategy, while the target, on which layouts depend, stays in the key (`MD3-06-same-bytes`: `enrang build --cpu=i386` and `enrang build --cpu=sse2 --float=sse2` write byte-identical interface files, where gcc 13 compiles one `double scale(double x, double k)` to `fldl` and `fmull` at `-march=i386` and to `movsd` and `mulsd` at `-march=pentium4 -mfpmath=sse`, since C ships machine code per level); and phase 4's level checks, EN0901, EN0910 and the float rules, are never cached: every command that reaches phase 4 runs them over the MIR of every module in the program, stored or freshly built (`MD3-06-cpu`: a sourceless library whose interface file `enrang build --cpu=i586` wrote is used by a program built with `--cpu=i386`; it compiles when its code has nothing above `i386`, and when its code holds `` asm(`cpuid`, ...) `` it is EN0901 at that instruction's stored span, with a note naming the library's interface file, never EN0315); the view fact of SPEC 4.10 is part of a function's declaration (`Q44-hash`: `mid` calls `lib`'s `show(sector.slice(0, 2))`, and `lib`'s `show` changes to print `sizeOf<T>()`, so it may no longer stand for a view; `mid` built from source rebuilds and is EN0401, `` `T` can't stand for the view `Slice<u8>` ``, and a `mid` with no source is EN0315, never rechecked, since a recheck reruns only phase 3; a forged interface file of `lib`, its key made to match, that states the fact for a `show` whose stored body uses `T` by value is EN0316); a leaf edit propagates through forwarding modules; `enrang check --cpu=i486` then `--cpu=i386` reports EN0901 both ways, since the level checks always run; test-item errors survive an existing interface file; each library item visible across rows has one contract, in build and test modes alike; each mark on a library stub equals the contract inferred from its body; a golden lists every library contract (`ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10).

## 19. How Windows, i386 and boot tests run here

**x86-64 Linux.** Native.

**i386-linux.** Natively through IA32 emulation (`CONFIG_IA32_EMULATION=y`). An ELF32 using `int 0x80` was built and executed here [measured]. A syscall-list test asserts `i386-linux` output uses only SPEC 8.4's old syscalls.

**Windows.** The real `.exe` runs from WSL through interop, written to the Linux filesystem. A hand-built PE32+ ran this way, and a spawn of `cmd.exe` through interop took 178 ms [measured], on Windows 10.0.26300. No Wine. Parallel interop throughput is measured at M7, and the cell's parallelism is set from that. PE structure is also checked by the own parser and `objdump -p`. Three extra tests cover what pipes and Linux cannot show:

- A 1 MiB local on the `win64` cell, plus an LIR golden of the descending probe order. Linux would hide a wrong order.
- The smallest possible program, with no statics and no fixups, runs through interop, because an empty `.data` section is refused by the loader [measured].
- Interop gives pipes, so `WriteConsoleW` never runs in the cell. A smoke test runs `cmd.exe /c start /wait /min x.exe` and asserts an exit code that encodes `WriteConsoleW`'s success.

**Boot without an emulator: the bench.** QEMU and OVMF are not installed, there is no `/dev/kvm`, and nothing else here can execute boot code or the 386 detection branches. So `enrang-testkit` contains `x86sim`, about 3,000 lines of test-only Rust:

- **Decoder and interpreter** for 16- and 32-bit floor forms, the x87 probe forms, and `cpuid`, `rdmsr`, `wrmsr` and `mov cr3`/`mov cr4`, written from the manuals and sharing no tables with `enrang-x86`. An instruction above the model's level raises #UD.
- **Descriptor caches,** because the worst boot bugs live there. All segment registers have cached base, limit, D/B and G. Decode width comes from CS.D. Caches survive clearing PE, so a protected-to-real toggle without 16-bit descriptors keeps decoding as 32-bit and fails. Limits raise #GP. Interrupts deliver through the IVT in real mode and the IDT in protected mode, and a triple fault is a test failure.
- **CPU models:** `i386-nofpu`, `i386-387`, `i386-287` (FPU probe passes, CR0.ET clear), `i486sx`, `i486dx`, `pentium`, `k6`, `p6`, `p4`. They differ in the AC toggle, ID toggle, CPUID table and FPU presence. `pentium` and `p6` return leaf-2 style data for out-of-range high leaves, as the SDM describes.
- **Scriptable BIOS:** INT 10h, 12h, 13h, 15h. Injects what QEMU cannot easily: no LBA extensions, `AH=41h` with CF=0 but no `AA55`, `AH=42h` failing where CHS works, `AH=08h` with SPT=0, int 13h errors N times, absent port `0x92`, stuck 8042, A20 already on, `AX=2401h` failing, no E820, no E801, an 8 GiB E820 table, overlapping and unsorted E820 entries, a non-text video mode, disk geometry.
- **Devices:** VGA text memory, mono adapter, COM1 as 8250, 16450, 16550, or absent, PIC, PIT, A20 gate with a configurable 8042 latency (including a retest that stays false for the first 20 ms), `isa-debug-exit`.

**Bench assertions (300 scenarios):**

- Each stage 1 and stage 2 path, every injected failure gives its error letter or falls back correctly. No path divides by zero.
- 8086 and 286 flag behaviour gives `C`.
- **Long-mode check.** A long-mode image on `i386-nofpu`, `pentium` and `p6` prints `L` in real mode, with no false LM from out-of-range leaves and no `cpuid` on the 386.
- **Protected-mode toggle.** A kernel at `--base=0x100000` loads through the toggle and runs. Negative control: a test-only image whose GDT lacks the 16-bit descriptors must hang or fault on the bench.
- **`--base`.** One scenario per rejected range of `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 9.6, plus `bss_end` past INT 12h top and past extended memory, which print `M`.
- **Memory view.** An 8 GiB E820 table, overlapping entries, and an allocator kernel filling all of low memory on the long-mode path with a page-table checksum assertion.
- **Kernel start.** Entered with an invalid GDTR, a garbage ESP and no magic in EAX, `_start` still zeroes BSS, loads its own GDT and delivers an exception to the early IDT. A panic before `BootInfo` is recorded uses `0xB8000` and no serial.
- **Interrupts.** IRQ0 through the remapped PIC into a stub, with full register and EFLAGS comparison. Vectors with and without error codes. A panic from a handler. IRQ0 fired inside `alloc`, with a handler that does not allocate, leaves the heap consistent.
- **Stack.** A 128 KB local on a 16 KB stack reports `stack overflow`. Every panic message at the limit stays inside the reserve.
- **`cpu.has` and SSE state.** Each combination of level, float strategy and model: `--cpu=i386 --float=soft` on `p4` reports `sse2` false until `cpu.enableSse()`; `--cpu=sse2 --float=soft` leaves EM clear and OSFXSR set; `--cpu=i586 --float=soft` on `pentium` leaves EM clear and reports `fpu` true. The `p4` scenario at `--cpu=sse2 --float=sse2` asserts CR0 and CR4 up to the first SSE instruction, then stops, because the bench has no SSE. `i386-287` reports `fpu` false.
- S9 produces its exact COM1 bytes, with the same text in VGA memory, on `i386-nofpu`.
- A `--cpu=i686` kernel on `i386-nofpu` prints `needs i686 (CMOV); this CPU lacks it` on VGA and COM1, then halts.
- Hello kernel runs in 640 KB with no extended memory; an allocator kernel runs in 2 MB.
- A stray x87 opcode under soft-float at `--cpu=i386` or `i486` traps through CR0.EM to the early IDT message.
- From M8, an `--float=x87` kernel on `i386-nofpu` stops at startup with its no-FPU message, as the level check does, and on `i386-387` it clears CR0.EM, which the scenario asserts before the FDIV check's first arithmetic instruction, where it stops. x87 arithmetic itself runs only under QEMU, or once `x86sim` decodes more than the probe forms.
- The `none-bench` conformance cell (section 12).
- From M10, stage 2's long-mode tail up to the far jump: page table contents, CR4, EFER, CR0 and selector `0x28`. The bench has no 64-bit interpreter.

**The bench proves logic, not hardware.** It shares its author's reading of the manuals. It is never counted as C3 or any other class, and the ledger shows it separately.

**Static image checks,** which run with no execution at all: floppy is exactly 1,474,560 bytes; `55 AA`; partition table fields; the six GDT descriptors of `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 9.6; `66 EA` far jump; patched parameters; stage budgets; two builds byte-identical; 16-bit `objdump` decode of stages. A corpus-level static test builds a 512-byte sector from user code with `--format=flat --base=0x7C00` and asserts `55 AA` at offset 510.

**UEFI memory map, no OVMF needed.** A unit test translates a synthetic OVMF-style memory map: only `EfiConventionalMemory` becomes usable.

**QEMU** (qemu-system-x86 8.2.2 from apt; TCG only). The owner approved installing it when M9 begins, and not before. Until it is present on a machine, these tests skip visibly:

```text
qemu-system-i386 -cpu 486 -m 32 -display none -no-reboot -serial stdio \
  -device isa-debug-exit,iobase=0xf4,iosize=4 -drive file=x.img,format=raw,if=ide
```

Use `if=floppy` for floppy images, and `-cpu core2duo` for `none-qemu-sse2`. Writing `0x10` to port `0xF4` exits with status 33, which counts as a pass. Any other status or a timeout fails. Serial bytes are compared with expected output. Tests repeat across available models. **QEMU evidence proves the boot flow, not level conformance.** Whether TCG rejects instructions above a model is unverified.

**OVMF, when installed.** The `efi-ovmf` cell puts the output at `EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` under a directory `$ESP`, since the prototype has no FAT writer, and runs the following. Confirm the OVMF path when the package is installed.

```text
qemu-system-x86_64 -bios /usr/share/ovmf/OVMF.fd -drive format=raw,file=fat:rw:$ESP \
  -serial stdio -display none -net none -no-reboot -device isa-debug-exit,iobase=0xf4,iosize=4
```

**Bochs,** not installed; the owner approved trying it when M9 begins. It is the only emulator route to executing the AC, ID and no-FPU branches. 386 emulation needs a custom build with `--enable-cpu-level=3` per older docs, unverified for 2.7. `ENRANG_BOCHS=<path>` enables those tests.

**Owner checklist for the Q8400 (C4), from M9.** The machine is an old Acer Aspire with legacy BIOS, confirmed by the owner, who does not know the exact model and has no other old machines, so this is the only real-hardware evidence the project can get:

1. `enrang build --target=i386-none --image=hdd kernel.en`, using S9.
2. Write the image to a USB stick with a Windows raw writer: Rufus in DD mode, or Win32 Disk Imager, reading the file through `\\wsl$\...`. In WSL2 a USB stick is not a visible raw block device without usbipd, so `dd` from where the image is built does not work; use `dd` only from a bare-metal Linux. The toolchain never writes to raw devices. Double-check the device name.
3. In the BIOS setup, enable `Legacy USB Support`, and select the USB-HDD boot entry.
4. Expect `Hello from Enrang` on screen. COM1 output needs a serial cable, if the board has a header.
5. Error letters, if one appears instead:

   | Letter | Meaning |
   |---|---|
   | `C` | CPU older than 386 |
   | `D` | Disk read failed through both LBA and CHS |
   | `A` | A20 could not be enabled |
   | `M` | Kernel does not fit in memory |
   | `L` | No long mode (64-bit images only) |

6. Record the result in `evidence/hardware.txt`: board, BIOS, image, what appeared, and any error letter. Only this file fills the C4 column.
7. Repeat at M10 with `x86_64-none --image=hdd`. This machine has no UEFI, so `--format=efi` cannot be tested on it: UEFI output runs only on OVMF, or on a newer PC with Secure Boot off.

## 20. Skip notices

A missing tool never fails silently and never fails the build by default.

```text
SKIP qemu-system-i386 not found: 212 tests not run
SKIP OVMF not found: 40 tests not run
SKIP as not found: 2400 oracle tests not run
```

- Every skip appears in the final summary and in the JSON evidence.
- `ENRANG_REQUIRE=qemu,as` turns the named skips into failures. A name matches exactly or as a prefix ending at `-`, ignoring case, so `qemu` covers `qemu-system-i386`.
- OVMF is a firmware file rather than a program, so a `PATH` lookup cannot find it; the OVMF cells check its path when they arrive at M10.
- `as`, `objdump`, `readelf`, `nm`, `gcc` and `rustc` may be used only as test-time oracles. Tests using them must skip visibly if absent. They are never required to compile an Enrang program, and the empty-`PATH` hermeticity test proves it.
- gdb, strace and valgrind are not installed and nothing depends on them.
- `/mnt/c` absent: Windows cell skips with a notice.

## 21. Evidence ledger and what cannot be verified here

**Ledger.** From M4, `enrang targets --evidence` prints `evidence/ledger.json`, which the matrix driver generates from test results, plus owner-entered `evidence/hardware.txt`. Four columns per CPU family: C1 level-proven, C2 run at that level on the dev host, C3 emulated naming the model, C4 real hardware. A class is printed only if reached, and the output says which commit proved it. Today C1, C2 and C3 read `planned`, and C4 reads `no hardware` for every family except the Q8400 and `Newer` rows, which read `planned`.

**Cannot be verified on this machine, today or by any test in this repository:**

- Any behaviour on a real 386, 486, Pentium, K5, K6, K7, P6, NetBurst, Atom or mobile chip. There is no such hardware. Running a level natively here proves the code, not the chip.
- Real AC, ID and no-FPU branches of detection, on real silicon. The i5-12400F takes the modern branch only. Bench or Bochs only.
- Real CPUID values of old chips. The bench models them from the matrix in `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 8.1, whose cells have mixed evidence.
- Errata behaviour: POPAD, F00F, FDIV, K6 stepping defects, 386 multiply.
- BIOS quirks: USB type guessing, BPB patching, A20 on real chipsets, 8042 timing, floppy timing.
- 3DNow!, which this host lacks. We never emit it.
- Boot on QEMU and UEFI on OVMF until they are installed, including GOP output and the framebuffer console.
- Whether stage 1 fits 350 bytes of code with the BIOS checks of `ARCHITECTURE.md` section 9.6, until it is assembled at M9.
- Stack probes and PE loader rules on any Windows build other than 10.0.26300, where they were measured.
- Early 64-bit parts lacking CMPXCHG16B and LAHF, beyond the static audit.

**Unverified facts carried in the design:** 486 CPUID per stepping, Prescott LAHF and CMPXCHG16B per stepping, Am386 POPAD behaviour, x87 clocks, x87 results on real old FPUs, the flawed Pentium's `1.333739068902037` for `4195835 / 3145727`, A-step CMPXCHG opcode, soft-float sizes. Docs must never claim real-chip verification that did not happen.

## 22. Speed

| Tier | Command | Budget | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| Default | `cargo test` | 60 s | In-process compiles, one shared parse of `core`, batched `as`, `vm64`, `lx64`, `l32-i386`, a tenth of generated volume |
| Full | `ENRANG_SCALE=10 cargo test` | 10 min | Full matrix |
| Nightly | `ENRANG_SCALE=100` | No hard limit | `rustc` twins, stepper, QEMU if present |

Exceeding a budget prints a warning and goes in the JSON evidence, because a wall-clock failure in `cargo test` would be flaky on WSL2 under load, in a suite whose principle is determinism. Only `ENRANG_REQUIRE=speed`, used on a controlled run, turns an overrun into a failure. Compiles run in-process through `enrang-driver`. For scale, `rustc` checked a 1,000-function file in 0.29 s and `/bin/true` spawned in 0.51 ms [both measured].

## 23. Definition of done per milestone

Every milestone also requires all earlier cells and meta-tests green, zero `rustc` warnings, and a runnable proof (`ARCHITECTURE.md` section 12). Counts are minimums at that milestone. "Every code" means every SPEC 13.2 row with `since` at or below that milestone.

| M | Gate |
|---|---|
| **M0** | Testkit self-tests; PRNG known answers; bless and orphan logic; code registry holds every SPEC 13.2 row with its phase, owner, `since` and fixture kind; `since` gating tested with a fake registry; renderer passes hand-built G01 shape; hermetic `Cargo.lock`; `explain` and `targets` goldens; deferred commands EN1301 and EN1302 goldens; feature and level tables tested against SPEC 10.1 |
| **M1** | 2,500 cases; 20k mutated files with no panic; format-reparse round trip on 2k generated programs; `enrang fmt` and `fmt --check` pass the four tests of section 17, with its Quotes and import-order cases; G01-G04 byte-exact; every `since(M1)` code; the tour and the `Ownership in three lines` block parse; parser fixtures for `a < b > (c)`, `a<b>(c)`, `f(a < b, c > (d))`, `x as usize < n`, `x as Box<i32>`; lexer fixtures for `0..n`, `[...a]`, `[....5]`, `a...b`; every EN1013 form of section 18, `[...a; 3]` and `[...0..n]` |
| **M2** | 2,500 more cases; Maranget against brute force; G05, G06, G12, G13, G17, G19, G20, G22 byte-exact; the spread typing and `Clone` rows of section 18; narrowed `for...of` sources typed once under the facts at the loop, and the state after the loop joined over its `break`s; SPEC 11 worked sizes for both `P`; every `since(M2)` code; every `C` row covered with an accept and a reject; the exact-satisfaction rule of SPEC 4.4 has a fixture per clause; the attack-review rows of section 18 whose codes are phase 2 |
| **M3** | 4,000 more cases, half negative with fixed siblings; MIR verifier after every pass, including a narrowed `for...of` head; `.mir` module round trip, with fixtures needing no front end; `remap_ids` text fixtures; G07-G11, G16, G18, G21, G23, G24 byte-exact; every row of the `for...of`, inferred-permission and attack-review tables of section 18, `f1`-`f6`, `h1`-`h8` and `l1`-`l10` included; `rustc` twin disagreements all named; every `since(M3)` code; every `H` row covered; drop-trace fixtures for the D2 temporary table and for partly moved `T \| null`, `Result` and a user enum |
| **M4** | 300 corpus programs on `vm64` and `vm32`, including those of section 18 for `for...of` and spread, and the printed output and `--oracle` runs of the attack-review rows; S1-S8, S10, S11 byte-exact; all accepted programs fault-free; every fault detector fires; every MIR statement kind hit; no `Asm` in the mono closure of S1-S8, S10 and S11; must-fail suite exact for each matcher and T code; leak gate on; reporters and snapshots golden; the interface-file meta-tests of section 18; VM speed measured and recorded |
| **M5** | All forms through byte oracle and level oracle, all exceptions recorded, no stale row; 500 corpus programs VM equals `lx64`; 10k `GenExpr` differential; `none`, `linear`, `chaos` agree; output audit on the corpus; hello ELF64 at most 12 KB; empty-`PATH` build; G15 byte-exact; `readelf` and own parser agree |
| **M6** | 400 reload scenarios; every `MIG`, `REF`, `FAIL` and `ADV` row covered, with the reload scenarios of section 18; worked scenario golden; each grant denied; refused means unchanged heap hash; `GenSchema` 2k walks; release output free of machinery; `@was` and `@devMigrate` inert byte-identically |
| **M7** | Full corpus through interop equals `lx64`; PE checks by own parser and `objdump -p`; only `kernel32.dll` imported; `.reloc` covers all absolute data (tested by loading with `DYNAMIC_BASE`); hello PE at most 16 KB; 1 MiB local and smallest-program tests pass; `WriteConsoleW` smoke test; parallel interop throughput measured |
| **M8** | Full corpus at all `l32-*` cells, the x87 cells included; audit at every level; 2M soft-float vectors bit-exact across the section 16 chain, x87 included; 100k `GenPair` vectors; old-syscall list; level diagnostics and clean-failure messages golden; stepper sample clean; allocator at most 2 KB; soft-float at most 16 KB; `core`+`alloc`+`os` no floats at most 32 KB; hello ELF32 at most 12 KB |
| **M9** | 300 bench scenarios, including the toggle's negative control; S9 exact on bench; `none-bench` cell green; `none-qemu-sse2` written and skipping visibly; static image checks; user-code boot sector test; floppy exactly 1,474,560 bytes; stage 1 at most 350 B code, stage 2 at most 4 KB, hello kernel at most 16 KB in 640 KB; G14 byte-exact; floor audit of kernel and stages; QEMU tests written and skipping visibly, run if installed; owner checklist published |
| **M10** | Static checks of `x86_64-none` images; bench runs the long-mode check on `i386-nofpu`, `pentium` and `p6`, then stage 2's tail up to the far jump, and asserts page tables, CR4, EFER, CR0 and selector `0x28`; UEFI memory-map translation tested with no OVMF; 64-bit kernel code runs only under QEMU, skipping visibly until then; audit at `generic64` rejects CMPXCHG16B, LAHF, SAHF, POPCNT in baseline; PE32+ EFI checks with mandatory `.reloc` and no imports; OVMF tests written and skipping visibly; ledger generated with every family's classes accurate |
